## 1. [[ROCm][Perf] Enable HCA dual-stream overlap for DeepSeek-V4](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56853)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56853
- **Author:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged By:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 23:46:01
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56853/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v4/amd/rocm.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR extends the existing ROCm multi-stream overlap logic in vllm/models/deepseek_v4/amd/rocm.py from CSA layers to HCA layers (compressor present, no indexer). Previously, when indexer was None, aux_stream_list was set to None, forcing HCA layers onto a single stream. Now aux streams are only disabled for dense layers (compressor is None), and a new HCA dual-stream fork runs the main compressor on aux stream 0 while the default stream produces q and inserts KV into the SWA cache, joining before sparse MLA attention. The gate was renamed from _enable_csa_multi_stream to _enable_multi_stream_overlap to reflect broader applicability.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change to the ROCm attention forward path for DeepSeek-V4 HCA layers, introducing a parallel fork/join inside the graph capture boundary. The default stream handles fused wqa/wkv GEMM, q/kv RMSNorm, wq_b, qnorm/RoPE, and SWA KV insert, while aux stream 0 runs the HCA main compressor. Reported gains are ~7.47% faster HCA layer decode, with end-to-end throughput improvements of 0.96%-3.43% and TPOT reductions of 0.96%-2.95% across concurrency levels, plus accuracy on GSM8K above the 94% threshold.

**Potential risks**  
The change relies on HIP event synchronization and static graph edges for correctness; the PR notes event waits can hang on ROCm in eager-break scenarios, which is why the gate restricts multi-stream to capture or non-dict metadata. The assert was relaxed from requiring both indexer and compressor to only compressor, so any future code path assuming indexer presence in this branch could break. The HCA fork assumes both branches only read hidden_states and their own weights/state with no cross-dependency before the join; any hidden shared state or ordering assumption would cause silent correctness issues. Gains are modest at high concurrency, and the change is ROCm/gfx950-specific with limited validation scope shown.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is that HCA main compressor work is independent of the query/KV chain and can be overlapped, mirroring the CSA optimization. The refactor cleanly separates the HCA dual-stream path from the CSA three-stream path via an early return, keeping the existing CSA logic intact. The gating logic correctly preserves the safety constraint that multi-stream only runs where fork/join becomes static graph edges, avoiding unreliable runtime HIP event sync. The PR is well-tested with benchmark and accuracy results, though the diff is small relative to the behavioral surface it touches.

## 2. [Revert "[Frontend] Omit absent fields from /inference/v1/generate stream chunks"](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57302)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57302
- **Author:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged By:** [vllm-bot](https://github.com/vllm-bot)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 23:31:59
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57302/files) (3):**
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/inference/generate.rs`
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/test_generate_stream.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/serving.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR reverts vllm-project/vllm#57264, which had made stream chunks omit all absent optional fields via exclude_none=True. The revert restores the previous behavior where only prompt_token_ids and mm_placeholders are conditionally excluded, while other None fields (usage, logprobs, finish_reason) are serialized as null again. The associated tests in both Rust and Python were removed.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the JSON serialization contract for /inference/v1/generate stream chunks: fields like usage, logprobs, and finish_reason will now appear as explicit nulls instead of being omitted. This affects both the Python serving layer (serving.py) and aligns with the Rust frontend behavior that was apparently not changed. Clients parsing these chunks must handle null values rather than missing keys.

**Potential risks**  
Downstream clients or tests that were updated to expect omitted fields (per the reverted PR) may break or behave incorrectly when they now receive explicit nulls. The revert removes test coverage for the omit-absent-fields contract, so regressions in serialization behavior could go undetected. There is also a risk of inconsistency if the Rust frontend and Python frontend diverge on this contract.

**Key insights**  
The revert suggests the original change caused problems, possibly breaking compatibility with clients or the Rust frontend that expects null fields. The remaining code comment still references Rust skipping None fields, but only applies to prompt metadata, indicating a partial and potentially inconsistent contract. Reviewers should verify whether the revert is intentional and whether the Rust and Python frontends are truly aligned after this change.

## 3. [[Frontend] Omit absent fields from /inference/v1/generate stream chunks](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57264)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57264
- **Author:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 22:58:57
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57264/files) (3):**
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/inference/generate.rs`
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/test_generate_stream.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/serving.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Python /inference/v1/generate streaming path now serializes data chunks with exclude_none=True instead of a per-field exclude set for prompt_token_ids and mm_placeholders, so absent optional fields (usage, logprobs, finish_reason, prompt_token_ids, mm_placeholders) are omitted rather than emitted as null. This aligns Python stream JSON with the Rust frontend's skip_serializing_none behavior and with the final usage chunk, and removes the special case introduced in #53187. Two new tests (Python and Rust) lock the wire-format contract.

**Technical impact**  
Wire format of Python stream chunks changes: previously-null keys are now absent. This is a behavior change for any client that indexes chunk["finish_reason"] or similar keys directly on non-final chunks; such clients must use .get(). The PR notes OpenAI-style clients and prime-rl's generate client already use .get(), and non-streaming responses are unchanged. The Rust change is test-only, adding a unit test that asserts the existing skip_serializing_none contract.

**Potential risks**  
Downstream consumers that assume fixed key presence (e.g., strict schema validation, direct dict indexing, or code that distinguishes null from missing) could break on Python stream chunks. The change is scoped to the token_in_token_out streaming path, but any client not covered by the PR's compatibility claim is a risk. Test coverage is limited to mocked engine outputs and a Rust serialization unit test; no end-to-end cross-frontend parity test is shown.

**Key insights**  
The change is small in code but semantically meaningful: it unifies serialization semantics across both frontends and removes a fragile per-field special case. The main review focus should be confirming no in-repo or documented client relies on null-valued keys in Python stream chunks, and that the new tests actually exercise the absent-field contract rather than just passing trivially. The Rust diff is test-only, so the only production behavior change is the three-line Python edit.

## 4. [[XPU] Enable XPU eplb](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/44987)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #44987
- **Author:** [mayuyuace](https://github.com/mayuyuace)
- **Merged By:** [jikunshang](https://github.com/jikunshang)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 22:19:14
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/44987/files) (16):**
  - `tests/conftest.py`
  - `tests/distributed/eplb_utils.py`
  - `tests/distributed/test_elastic_ep.py`
  - `tests/distributed/test_eplb_algo.py`
  - `tests/distributed/test_eplb_events.py`
  - `tests/distributed/test_eplb_execute.py`
  - `tests/distributed/test_eplb_fused_moe_layer.py`
  - `tests/distributed/test_eplb_fused_moe_layer_dep_nvfp4.py`
  - `vllm/config/parallel.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/eplb/async_worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/eplb/eplb_communicator.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/eplb/eplb_state.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/eplb/eplb_utils.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/eplb/rebalance_execute.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/experts/xpu_moe.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/router/base_router.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR enables Expert Parallel Load Balancing (EPLB) on Intel XPU devices by adding a new TorchDistXCCLStagedEplbCommunicator backend and generalizing CUDA-specific code paths to be platform-agnostic. It replaces hardcoded torch.cuda.Stream/Event/stream() calls with torch.Stream/torch.Event/torch.accelerator equivalents, introduces a device_stream context manager, and updates config validation to allow XPU. Tests are updated to parametrize over cuda/xpu and to use gloo CPU groups for XPU tensor gathering since gloo does not support XPU tensors.

**Technical impact**  
Adds a new 'torch_xccl' EPLB communicator option and auto-selects it on XPU, while preserving existing CUDA/ROCm behavior via torch.accelerator abstractions. The device_stream helper and renamed stream parameters (cuda_stream -> stream) touch the async EPLB worker, communicator, state, and rebalance execution paths, so the change is broad across the EPLB subsystem. Test infrastructure now supports XPU by using accelerator-agnostic device selection and backend selection (xccl vs nccl).

**Potential risks**  
The refactor from torch.cuda.* to torch.accelerator.* APIs assumes these APIs are available and behave equivalently across all supported PyTorch versions and platforms; any version mismatch could break CUDA paths. The new TorchDistXCCLStagedEplbCommunicator is only exercised on XPU hardware, so CUDA CI will not validate it. Changes to test helpers (e.g., switching ep_group from cpu_group to device_group in some tests) could alter semantics on CUDA and may mask or introduce regressions. The xpu_moe.py change passing expert_map to the fused MoE impl could affect XPU MoE correctness and needs validation.

**Key insights**  
The PR is a well-structured platform generalization: it centralizes stream handling via device_stream and torch.accelerator, minimizing per-platform branching. The XPU-specific gloo limitation (no XPU tensor support) is handled by staging gathers on CPU, which is a reasonable workaround. The auto-selection logic in ParallelConfig.__post_init__ correctly prioritizes torch_xccl on XPU before falling back to nixl/pynccl on CUDA. The included Qwen3-30B-A3B gsm8k lm_eval results provide some functional evidence, but broader XPU regression coverage would strengthen confidence.

## 5. [[Feature][Multimodal] Add cross-encoder caching to Mooncake P2P](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56242)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56242
- **Author:** [jiaran-king](https://github.com/jiaran-king)
- **Merged By:** [ZJY0516](https://github.com/ZJY0516)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 22:16:42
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56242/files) (15):**
  - `docs/features/cross_encoder_cache.md`
  - `docs/features/disagg_encoder.md`
  - `tests/v1/ec_connector/unit/test_cross_encoder_cache.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_mooncake_store_worker.py`
  - `vllm/config/ec_transfer.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/mooncake/config.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/mooncake/metadata.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/mooncake/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/mooncake/worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/mooncake_store_embedding/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/mooncake_store_embedding/backend.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/mooncake_store_embedding/data.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/mooncake_store_embedding/store_client.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/mooncake/store/worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/mooncake_store.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds cross-Encoder output reuse for multimodal EPD serving by caching Encoder embeddings in a shared Mooncake Store. A new mooncake_store_embedding package (backend resolver/publisher, store client, data/key helpers) is wired into the Mooncake EC scheduler and worker, gated by a new cross_encoder_cache config flag with validation. MooncakeStoreConfig and setup logic are extracted from the KV connector worker into a shared vllm/distributed/mooncake_store.py module, and docs plus extensive unit tests are added.

**Technical impact**  
Encoder workers now perform a synchronous Store lookup before encoding and asynchronously publish computed outputs, skipping Encoder forward on hits while retaining existing ECMooncakeConnector P2P delivery to Prefill. The refactor centralizes Mooncake Store config/setup so EC and KV paths share it, and introduces new config knobs (store_max_pending_items, store_max_pending_bytes, store_read_buffer_bytes) plus startup validation requiring MRv2, Encoder-only producer, no LoRA, and mm_processor_cache_gb > 0. Reported controlled results show large TTFT reductions under Encoder reassignment workloads.

**Potential risks**  
The Store client uses native Mooncake APIs with careful error-code handling; unconfirmed I/O poisons clients and retains buffers, and unexpected native errors can fail the worker, so correctness depends on the exact Mooncake version (0.3.12+) and error-code semantics. Publication is best-effort and asynchronous, so concurrent cold requests may duplicate computation, and incompatible cached objects can cause repeated fallback until evicted. The shared-config extraction touches the KV connector worker and could regress existing KV Store behavior if any subtle setup differences exist. Performance claims are from limited single-run experiments and the prototype was not re-measured after the final refactor.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped to Mooncake and gated behind an opt-in flag, with substantial unit coverage of codec, chunking, failure, and ownership paths. The main review focus should be the native I/O safety contract (poisoning, buffer retention, error-code classification), the shared MooncakeStoreConfig refactor's impact on the KV connector, and the correctness of content-identifier/namespace construction for cross-Encoder reuse. The PR description is unusually thorough on limitations and validation, which helps assess residual risk.

## 6. [[ROCm][Bugfix] Reduce CUDA graph divergences](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57229)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57229
- **Author:** [mawong-amd](https://github.com/mawong-amd)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 22:12:13
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57229/files) (5):**
  - `tests/test_config.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/parallel_state.py`
  - `vllm/platforms/rocm.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu_model_runner.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu_worker.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR reverts several ROCm-specific workarounds that are no longer needed: it removes the ROCm-only graph capture on the current stream in profile_cudagraph_memory (reverting #48526), removes the ROCm DS V4 fallback to CUDAGraphMode.NONE when MRV2 is enabled (reverting part of #55095), and gates torch.compile specialization warmups on the presence of a compiled submodule in addition to VLLM_COMPILE mode. The stated goal is to reduce ROCm/CUDA divergences as a cleaner base for ModelRunner V2 default enablement on ROCm.

**Technical impact**  
graph_capture() loses its optional graph_capture_context parameter and always allocates a fresh side stream, so ROCm profiling graphs now use a side stream like CUDA. The ROCm platform default that forced CUDAGraphMode.NONE for DeepseekV4 architectures under MRV2 is deleted, and the corresponding ROCm-specific test is removed. compile_or_warm_up_model now skips specialization warmups when no submodule is actually torch.compiled, avoiding unnecessary work.

**Potential risks**  
Re-enabling side-stream capture on ROCm could reintroduce the cross-stream wait defect if the referenced fixes (#53712, #55099) are not fully effective, potentially causing hangs or performance regressions. Removing the DS V4 eager fallback may resurface the accuracy issue from #52644 on gfx950/MRV2, though the PR claims it no longer applies and reports gsm8k accuracy of 0.954-0.972. The new has_compiled_submodule gate could skip warmups in cases where a compiled submodule is created lazily, potentially missing specialization coverage.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-justified with before/after gsm8k accuracy and TP8 performance measurements across multiple concurrencies, showing no regression. The removal of the ROCm-specific test is appropriate since the behavior it covered is being deleted. The change to gate warmups on has_compiled_submodule is a sensible optimization but its interaction with lazy compilation should be verified in CI.

## 7. [[CI] Fix ruff docstring violations in EC connector files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57283)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57283
- **Author:** [khluu](https://github.com/khluu)
- **Merged By:** [khluu](https://github.com/khluu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 21:13:16
- **Type:** `TRIVIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57283/files) (2):**
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/base.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/metrics.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR applies purely mechanical ruff auto-fixes to two EC connector files to resolve pydocstyle D200 violations. The violations arose from merge skew: PR #52136 enabled pydocstyle (D) rules while PR #54960 added non-compliant docstrings and was merged without rebasing. The changes move opening triple quotes onto the same line as the first docstring text and collapse single-line docstrings.

**Technical impact**  
No behavioral or semantic change. Only docstring formatting is altered, which does not affect runtime logic, imports, or public API signatures. The fix restores green pre-commit ruff-check and ruff-format runs on main.

**Potential risks**  
Minimal risk. The only conceivable concern is accidental modification of docstring content during reformatting, but the diff shows text is preserved verbatim with only quote placement changed. No functional code paths are touched.

**Key insights**  
This is a CI hygiene fix addressing merge skew between a linting-rule PR and a feature PR. It highlights the value of rebasing before merge when lint rules change on main, and confirms the changes are safe, mechanical, and AI-generated with no semantic edits.

## 8. [[A2A] Allow EPLB + SharedExpert Overlap + DeepEPv2](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57236)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57236
- **Author:** [robertgshaw2-redhat](https://github.com/robertgshaw2-redhat)
- **Merged By:** [robertgshaw2-redhat](https://github.com/robertgshaw2-redhat)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 21:02:40
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57236/files) (2):**
  - `vllm/distributed/device_communicators/all2all.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/runner/shared_experts.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR adds deepep_v2 to the _EPLB_OVERLAP_SAFE_BACKENDS tuple in shared_experts.py, enabling shared expert overlap when EPLB is active with the deepep_v2 all2all backend. It also adds an EP_DISABLE_GIN environment variable check in all2all.py's _check_gin_support to skip the GIN requirement probe, mirroring the exemption previously granted to flashinfer_nvlink_one_sided. The stated goal is to allow EPLB plus shared expert overlap on deepep_v2 without accuracy regression.

**Technical impact**  
The shared_experts.py change alters runtime behavior by permitting concurrent shared-expert computation and EPLB rebalancing on deepep_v2, which can improve throughput by overlapping communication and compute. The all2all.py change short-circuits a GIN capability check when EP_DISABLE_GIN is set, affecting communicator initialization validation. Both changes are small but behavior-affecting, and the GIN skip is broader than the stated PR scope since it is not gated to deepep_v2 specifically.

**Potential risks**  
The EP_DISABLE_GIN bypass in _check_gin_support is a general escape hatch that could mask genuine GIN misconfiguration for other backends or deployments, potentially leading to silent correctness or performance issues. The overlap enablement relies on validation on a single node with 8xH200 and DeepSeek-V2-Lite; other models, topologies, or CUDA-graph-enabled paths may behave differently. The PR description itself notes a known unrelated garbage-output issue with deepep_v2 and CUDA graphs when EP_DISABLE_GIN=1, which complicates confidence in the tested configuration.

**Key insights**  
The change is a targeted one-line allowlist addition plus a small environment-variable bypass, validated with GSM8K accuracy parity across 52 async rebalances and no hangs. The GIN skip is arguably out of scope for a PR titled about EPLB overlap and should be reviewed separately for safety. Because sequence-parallel disables shared expert overlap, the effective impact is limited to TP=1 deployments, narrowing the blast radius but also limiting test coverage of the overlap path.

## 9. [[ROCm][CI] Fix Nixl+Offloading PD edge cases on TheRock image](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57249)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57249
- **Author:** [mawong-amd](https://github.com/mawong-amd)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 20:56:14
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57249/files) (2):**
  - `docker/Dockerfile.rock`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/nixl_integration/test_multi_connector_edge_cases.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR addresses two issues in the ROCm CI Nixl+Offloading PD edge case tests. First, it bumps AINIC_VERSION in docker/Dockerfile.rock from 1.117.3-hydra to 1.117.5-a-77 to match docker/Dockerfile.rocm and eliminate UCX 'arm iface returned Success' log spam. Second, it appends a deterministic counting instruction to the eviction prompt in test_multi_connector_edge_cases.py so direct text matching is not defeated by tiny hot-vs-cold prefix cache numerical shifts.

**Technical impact**  
The Dockerfile change updates the AINIC driver/library version used in TheRock images, aligning it with the main ROCm image and requiring ionic-dkms 26.03.3.001. The test change makes the eviction prompt continuation deterministic enough for exact output matching while preserving the test's intent of exercising prefix cache hot-vs-cold paths. Both changes are limited to CI/infra and test code, with no production runtime behavior change.

**Potential risks**  
The AINIC version bump could introduce compatibility issues with host drivers that were only validated against the older 25.11.1.001 ionic-dkms, potentially affecting users building TheRock images. The test prompt modification could mask genuine regressions in prefix cache correctness if the new continuation is too trivial, though the PR argues the eviction prompt itself is not what is under test. The PR notes verification is still pending on ROCm 7.2 and CUDA paths.

**Key insights**  
The failing test was root-caused to a near-tie (margin 0.0625 on ROCm 10, exact tie on ROCm 7.2) between top-2 next-token candidates, which sits inside the measured 0.005-0.06 logit shift caused by prefix cache hot-vs-cold paths. This is a legitimate numerical divergence rather than a bug, so the fix appropriately makes the test continuation deterministic instead of loosening the assertion. The AINIC bump mirrors prior updates in PRs 54112 and 56459, keeping TheRock consistent with the main ROCm Dockerfile.

## 10. [[Rust Frontend] Bump vllm-proto to 0.3.0](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57233)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57233
- **Author:** [alec-flowers](https://github.com/alec-flowers)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 20:41:56
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57233/files) (3):**
  - `rust/Cargo.lock`
  - `rust/proto/Cargo.toml`
  - `rust/proto/README.md`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Bumps the vllm-proto crate from 0.2.0 to 0.3.0 across rust/proto/Cargo.toml, rust/Cargo.lock, and the README dependency example. The bump is needed because a new generated public struct field (ParallelismInfo.data_parallel_size_local from #57116) can break Rust struct literals even though the protobuf change is wire-compatible, so a pre-1.0 minor version increment signals the breaking API change to downstream consumers like Dynamo.

**Technical impact**  
This is a release metadata and documentation change only; no serving or model behavior is affected. It updates the crate version, the workspace lockfile entry, and the README example to 0.3. Merging alone does not publish; a maintainer must push the proto-v0.3.0 tag on the merged commit to trigger the crates.io publishing workflow.

**Potential risks**  
Downstream consumers pinning vllm-proto = 0.2 will not automatically pick up the new field and must update their dependency, which is the intended signal of the breaking change. If the proto-v0.3.0 tag is not pushed, the release will not reach crates.io and downstream users will be blocked. Validation was performed only against a specific upstream revision, so compatibility with other vLLM revisions is not established.

**Key insights**  
The change is small in diff size but semantically significant because it gates a downstream-facing API addition behind a version bump. The author correctly identified that adding a public struct field is a breaking change for Rust struct literals despite protobuf wire compatibility. The dry-run publish and grpc test suite passing provide reasonable confidence, but the actual publish step remains a manual maintainer action after merge.

  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/offloading_connector/test_config.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/offloading_connector/test_worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/offloading/config.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/offloading/worker.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR fixes issue #56772 by enabling a single CPU copy for certified canonical MLA/DSA caches in the KV offloading connector. It adds a replicated_layout condition in config.py for canonical layouts with MLA specs, updates worker.py so all ranks act as store writers when canonical_layout is set, and adds tests covering TP independence and writer submission behavior.

**Technical impact**  
The change makes canonical MLA/DSA offloading layouts parallelism-agnostic by forcing tp_size=1 and replicated_layout=True in the FileMapper run config, allowing compatible TP sizes to share equal P2P row lengths. Worker store-writer selection is broadened to include canonical layouts, ensuring all ranks participate in storing rather than only rank 0. Tests verify behavior across TP sizes 1, 2, and 4 with and without scheduler config.

**Potential risks**  
The new replicated_layout branch requires all selected group specs to be exactly MLAAttentionSpec, nnodes_within_dp == 1, and either world_size == 1 or an mp backend; other backends or mixed spec types silently fall back to prior behavior. Broadening _is_store_writer to all ranks under canonical_layout could increase redundant store submissions if the underlying store path is not idempotent, though tests suggest this is intended. The change is gated on canonical_layout and is_parallelism_agnostic, limiting blast radius.

**Key insights**  
The fix hinges on the observation that canonical MLA/DSA rows are TP-independent, so a single CPU copy suffices and all ranks can safely act as writers. The added test test_canonical_mla_dsa_rows_are_tp_independent directly asserts tp_size==1 and replicated_layout==True, which is the core invariant. The parametrized writer test confirms rank 1 with canonical_layout now submits stores, matching the new worker logic.

## 12. [[CI] Support `FORCE_COLOR` env var; enable colors in CPU CI output](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56913)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56913
- **Author:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged By:** [tlrmchlsmth](https://github.com/tlrmchlsmth)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 19:40:22
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56913/files) (5):**
  - `.buildkite/scripts/hardware_ci/run-cpu-test-arm.sh`
  - `.buildkite/scripts/hardware_ci/run-cpu-test.sh`
  - `tests/test_logger.py`
  - `vllm/envs.py`
  - `vllm/logger.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds support for the de-facto standard FORCE_COLOR environment variable to force ANSI colored logging output even when stdout is not a TTY, complementing the existing VLLM_LOGGING_COLOR flag. The CPU CI scripts now export TERM, PY_COLORS, FORCE_COLOR, and CLICOLOR_FORCE so CI logs render with colors, and a new unit test covers the precedence rules.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a new FORCE_COLOR env var in vllm/envs.py and wires it into _use_color() in vllm/logger.py, where it forces color unless NO_COLOR or VLLM_LOGGING_COLOR=0 override it. FORCE_COLOR is also added to the compile_factors set, so it participates in compilation cache keying. CI container invocations gain four new environment variables affecting terminal/color behavior.

**Potential risks**  
The FORCE_COLOR parsing treats any value other than '0' as true, so FORCE_COLOR=0 disables forcing but FORCE_COLOR=false or empty string would still enable it, which may diverge from the Node.js convention where '0' and 'false' disable color. Adding FORCE_COLOR to compile_factors changes cache keys, potentially invalidating existing compiled caches. Forcing color in CI could emit ANSI escape codes into log artifacts that downstream tooling may not parse cleanly.

**Key insights**  
The change is small but behavior-affecting, so it is correctly classified as substantial rather than trivial. Test coverage validates the intended precedence (NO_COLOR and VLLM_LOGGING_COLOR=0 win over FORCE_COLOR), which is the most important correctness property. Reviewers should confirm the FORCE_COLOR truthiness semantics match expectations and that cache-key inclusion is intentional.

## 13. [[EC Connector] Add Metrics Collection](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54960)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #54960
- **Author:** [omerpaz95](https://github.com/omerpaz95)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 19:36:23
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54960/files) (12):**
  - `tests/v1/core/test_scheduler.py`
  - `tests/v1/ec_connector/unit/test_ec_connector_metrics.py`
  - `tests/v1/ec_connector/unit/test_ec_output_aggregator.py`
  - `tests/v1/ec_connector/unit/test_worker_ec_connector.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/base.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/metrics.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/ec_transfer/ec_connector/utils.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/metrics/loggers.py`
  - `vllm/v1/metrics/stats.py`
  - `vllm/v1/outputs.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/gpu/ec_connector.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR ports the KV connector metrics infrastructure to the EC (Encoder Cache) connector, adding ECConnectorStats, ECConnectorLogging, ECConnectorPromMetrics, and ECConnectorProm in a new metrics.py, plus no-op hooks on ECConnectorBase, a new ec_connector_stats field on ECConnectorOutput and SchedulerStats, worker-side population, output aggregation, scheduler-side aggregation, and CLI/Prometheus logger wiring. The motivation is that EC had no telemetry path at all, while KV already had a proven pattern to mirror.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces new public-ish APIs (get_ec_connector_stats, build_ec_connector_stats, build_prom_metrics) that default to None/no-op, so existing connectors are unaffected. The ECOutputAggregator change is behaviorally meaningful: worker-reported EC stats are now folded across ranks before reaching the scheduler, fixing a latent drop in the multiprocess executor path. SchedulerStats gains a new optional field, and both LoggingStatLogger and PrometheusStatLogger now observe EC stats when present, gated on ec_transfer_config being set.

**Potential risks**  
The aggregation logic in ECOutputAggregator and scheduler.update_from_output mirrors KV but is new code; subtle ordering or empty-stats handling bugs could surface with real connectors. The scheduler-side aggregation calls get_ec_connector_stats() on every update_from_output when an EC connector exists, which could add overhead or raise if a connector implements it poorly. Prometheus metric registration depends on connector_cls.build_prom_metrics returning a valid object; a misbehaving connector could break logger init. No concrete connector implements these hooks yet, so end-to-end behavior is only validated by unit tests and mocks.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped as base infrastructure with safe defaults, and the test coverage directly exercises the no-op paths, the aggregator fold order, and the scheduler-side worker/scheduler stats combination. The most consequential non-cosmetic fix is the ECOutputAggregator change, which prevents worker stats from being silently dropped. Reviewers should focus on the scheduler aggregation branch and the Prometheus/CLI wiring for parity with the KV implementation, since those are the only places where behavior changes for existing deployments.

## 14. [[Core] Don't issue a blocking collective RPC during engine handshake](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57226)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57226
- **Author:** [okorzh-amd](https://github.com/okorzh-amd)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 19:30:59
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57226/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/engine/test_engine_core_client.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/core.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
EngineCoreProc._make_ready_response() previously always called Executor.supports_draft_weight_updates(), a collective_rpc with no timeout, which could block the engine handshake before ready_event.set() and cause a spurious AssertionError crash at data_parallel_size=1. The change guards that call behind a check for vllm_config.weight_transfer_config, since Worker.supports_draft_weight_updates() only returns True when a weight-transfer engine exists, which requires that config. Two unit tests were added to pin both the skipped and queried paths.

**Technical impact**  
Removes an avoidable engine-to-worker round trip from the pre-ready startup path when weight transfer is not configured, eliminating a class of startup stalls and the misleading 'Failed core proc(s): {}' front-end error. Behavior is unchanged when weight_transfer_config is set, so the draft-weight-update capability is still reported correctly in that case. The change is small and localized to v1/engine/core.py plus tests.

**Potential risks**  
The guard assumes supports_draft_weight_updates() can only be True when weight_transfer_config is not None; if a future executor implementation returns True without that config, the ready response would incorrectly report False. The underlying timeout=10 wait and bare assert in EngineCoreProc.__init__ remain unaddressed, so other pre-ready blocking calls could still trigger the same crash. Tests rely on MagicMock and may not catch real executor/worker divergence.

**Key insights**  
The fix targets the root cause of a specific startup stall rather than the symptom, and the PR explicitly scopes out the timeout/assert trap for separate work. The complementary relationship with PR #48871 (front-end diagnostics) is well articulated, and both touch the same test file, so a trivial rebase will be needed for whichever lands second. The added tests correctly fail before and pass after, giving good regression coverage for the changed branch.

## 15. [[Scheduler] Add --max-num-active-seqs to cap RUNNING admission](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56758)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56758
- **Author:** [ChuanLi1101](https://github.com/ChuanLi1101)
- **Merged By:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 19:22:12
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56758/files) (5):**
  - `tests/v1/core/test_scheduler.py`
  - `tests/v1/core/utils.py`
  - `vllm/config/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/engine/arg_utils.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new optional SchedulerConfig.max_num_active_seqs / --max-num-active-seqs flag that decouples the RUNNING admission cap from max_num_seqs, which previously served both as the model-runner sizing parameter and the admission limit. The scheduler now uses max_num_active_reqs (defaulting to max_num_running_reqs when unset) in the WAITING admission loop, with validation that it must be <= max_num_seqs. A unit test and test helper plumbing were added.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior is unchanged by default since None falls back to max_num_seqs. When set, fewer requests are admitted into RUNNING while model runner buffers, KV sizing, and CUDA graph capture still follow max_num_seqs, allowing smaller decode batches without recapturing graphs. The admission check correctly accounts for streaming slots via num_waiting_for_streaming_input, and no preemption is introduced, so lowering the cap takes effect only as requests finish.

**Potential risks**  
The new cap is enforced only in the scheduler's WAITING admission loop, so other code paths that assume len(running) can reach max_num_seqs (metrics, logging, memory profiling, or downstream components) may need review. Validation only checks max_num_active_seqs <= max_num_seqs; interaction with max_num_batched_tokens and chunked prefill is not explicitly validated. Test coverage is a single unit test and does not exercise streaming-slot accounting, preemption, or end-to-end serving behavior.

**Key insights**  
The change cleanly separates runner capacity from admission policy, which is a reasonable and low-risk refactor given the None default preserves existing behavior. The naming distinction between max_num_running_reqs (runner slots) and max_num_active_reqs (admission) is clear and well documented. Reviewers should confirm no other scheduler or engine code relies on running reaching max_num_seqs, and consider adding tests for the streaming-input interaction and the validation error path.

## 16. [[Test][Core] Add hybrid model prefix cache hit-rate coverage](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57127)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57127

  - `tests/v1/core/prefix_cache/test_hybrid_prefix_cache_hit_rate.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new end-to-end test file (tests/v1/core/prefix_cache/test_hybrid_prefix_cache_hit_rate.py, +156 lines) that exercises multi-turn prefix-cache reuse for the hybrid model Qwen/Qwen3.5-0.8B, both with and without MTP. It runs eight concurrent three-turn conversations, replays the original request histories, and asserts per-turn cached-token hit-rate floors (90% base, 75% MTP) plus exact agreement between accumulated chat usage and Prometheus prefix-cache counters.

**Technical impact**  
Test-only change with no runtime behavior impact. It adds coverage for serving-API-level multi-turn prefix caching, cache-salt session isolation, MTP re-prefill behavior, and metric accounting consistency, complementing existing scheduler- and cache-manager-level tests. It relies on RemoteOpenAIServer, --enable-prompt-tokens-details, and optional --speculative-config MTP settings.

**Potential risks**  
The test is GPU-dependent and uses fixed thresholds that could become flaky if model, tokenizer, or cache block sizing changes; the PR notes thresholds were tuned so one additional missed block fails. It also depends on Prometheus metric names and asynchronous metric propagation, mitigated by a 30s polling wait. The description states human line-by-line review and independent verification are still pending.

**Key insights**  
The test design is strong: it replays original histories to avoid generated-reply divergence affecting measured prompts, isolates sessions with UUID cache salts, and cross-checks client-reported usage against server metrics. The author validated thresholds on GB200 hardware and with an HTTP mock driver, showing 89%/74% hits fail and zero/missing/overcounted metrics fail. Since it is a new test rather than docs or formatting, it is classified as SUBSTANTIAL despite no production behavior change.

## 17. [Use skip-redaction option for crcr-report](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56532)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56532
- **Author:** [atalman](https://github.com/atalman)
- **Merged By:** [khluu](https://github.com/khluu)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 19:01:55
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56532/files) (1):**
  - `.buildkite/scripts/crcr-report.sh`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The crcr-report.sh Buildkite script now passes --skip-redaction to both `buildkite-agent secret get` and `buildkite-agent oidc request-token`, and updates the accompanying comments that previously claimed the flag could not help. This is needed because the docker plugin does not expose BUILDKITE_AGENT_JOB_API_SOCKET to the container, so any Job API client construction (used for redaction) fails; on agent v3.107.0 the client is built inside the SkipRedaction guard, allowing the flag to bypass it. The change makes the nightly CRCR report actually post instead of silently skipping.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change in CI tooling: the secret and OIDC token are now fetched without being registered with the log redactor. The flag is placed before the positional key because urfave/cli v1 stops parsing flags at the first positional argument. The script remains gated on TORCH_NIGHTLY=1 and main, stays best-effort with exit 0 on failures, and still prefers BUILDKITE_API_TOKEN from the environment. It depends on ci-infra PR #554 pinning the agent to >= 3.107.0.

**Potential risks**  
Skipping redaction means the secret and OIDC token are no longer registered with the log redactor, so any accidental echo or set -x would leak them; the PR asserts neither occurs, but this is a real regression in defense-in-depth. The change also hard-depends on agent >= 3.107.0, so if the ci-infra pin is not deployed first, the secret get call will fail as before and the OIDC call may break. The OIDC call's 2>/dev/null still hides errors, so failures surface only as the generic 'could not mint a Buildkite OIDC token' message.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is an ordering bug in older buildkite-agent versions where the Job API client was constructed before the SkipRedaction check, making the flag ineffective under the docker plugin. The fix is tightly coupled to an external infra upgrade (ci-infra #554), so the PR is not self-contained and must land after the agent pin. The comment correction is important because the old comment actively discouraged the correct fix, which explains why the nightly has been skipping since #54605.

## 18. [[Bugfix][Rust Frontend] Accept appended EngineCoreOutput fields](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56533)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56533
- **Author:** [alec-flowers](https://github.com/alec-flowers)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 18:54:44
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56533/files) (5):**
  - `rust/src/engine-core-client/src/protocol/mod.rs`
  - `rust/src/engine-core-client/src/protocol/output.rs`
  - `rust/src/engine-core-client/src/protocol/serde_utils.rs`
  - `rust/src/engine-core-client/src/tests/client.rs`
  - `rust/src/engine-core-client/src/tests/python_compat.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new AllowTrailingFields serde adapter in serde_utils.rs and opts the WireEngineCoreOutputs.outputs field into it via serde_as, so the Rust decoder tolerates appended fields on EngineCoreOutput arrays (e.g. vLLM-Omni extensions) instead of failing with an incorrect-length error. Tests and a Python msgspec fixture were added to cover the new behavior.

**Technical impact**  
Changes deserialization behavior for the output envelope: known fields are still validated and defaulted by the derived Deserialize_tuple, while unknown trailing elements are consumed with IgnoredAny. This aligns Rust decoding with msgspec's append-only schema evolution and unblocks ordinary inference when Omni appends fields, without adding dependencies or altering serialization or other protocol structs.

**Potential risks**  
The adapter is generic and could be misapplied to schemas where trailing fields are not truly append-only, silently masking schema drift or ordering changes. It only affects the outputs vector in WireEngineCoreOutputs; standalone EngineCoreOutput deserialization and serialization remain strict, so behavior is asymmetric. No GPU/Omni serving validation was performed, so real Omni payload handling is unverified.

**Key insights**  
The fix is narrowly scoped to the output envelope and reuses the existing derived decoder rather than duplicating the schema or building a dynamic tree. Tests confirm nested unknown values, MessagePack extensions, multiple outputs, and continued rejection of malformed known fields. Sensitivity checks show the adapter is required for the Python fixture to pass, and the change is explicitly not full Omni serving support.

## 19. [[ROCm][Bugfix] Ignore descales for unquantized AITER caches](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56726)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56726
- **Author:** [dongluw](https://github.com/dongluw)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 18:29:51
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56726/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_attention_backends.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/rocm_aiter_fa.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a ROCm AITER attention bug where K/V scale tensors were passed to decode kernels even for unquantized KV caches, causing AITER to treat stale non-neutral values (e.g. from dummy-weight init) as descales and corrupt attention output (InfoVQA score dropped from 0.952 to 0.000). The fix introduces a _get_kv_cache_descales helper that returns None for unquantized caches and expanded descales only for quantized (FP8) caches, and constructs them only in the unified-attention branches. A new ROCm-specific correctness test with non-neutral scale tensors on an unquantized sliding-window cache was added.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change in vllm/v1/attention/backends/rocm_aiter_fa.py: unquantized KV cache decode paths now pass k_descale=None/v_descale=None to AITER kernels, while FP8 paths retain expanded descales. The refactor also avoids creating unused eager tensor views in ordinary paged-attention decode, and the test harness (MockAttentionLayer, run_attention_backend, _test_backend_correctness) now supports configurable layer scales and handles backends that return output directly.

**Potential risks**  
The change relies on is_quantized_kv_cache correctly classifying all quantized dtypes; any quantized path not covered could silently lose descales. The descale_shape now uses self.num_kv_heads instead of key_cache.shape[2], which should be equivalent but assumes consistency. Test coverage is limited to ROCm/AITER environments, so regressions on other backends or non-AITER ROCm paths would not be caught by this test.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a semantic mismatch: AITER interprets non-null descales as a dequantization instruction, so passing neutral-looking but stale scale tensors silently corrupts unquantized attention. The fix is minimal and targeted, gating descale construction on kv_cache_dtype and consolidating logic into a helper. The added test with non-neutral scales (0.25/0.5) directly reproduces the failure mode and validates the fix on MI300X.

## 20. [[A2A] Add DeepEPv2 to SP Supported List](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57210)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57210
- **Author:** [robertgshaw2-redhat](https://github.com/robertgshaw2-redhat)
- **Merged By:** [robertgshaw2-redhat](https://github.com/robertgshaw2-redhat)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 18:29:11
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57210/files) (3):**
  - `vllm/config/parallel.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/all2all_utils.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/prepare_finalize/deepep_v2.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR adds DeepEPv2 to the sequence-parallel (SP) supported MoE all2all backends. It registers 'deepep_v2' in the use_sequence_parallel_moe allowlist, passes moe.moe_parallel_config.sp_size into the DeepEPv2 prepare/finalize constructor, and uses that sp_size to divide the per-rank token count via cdiv when computing num_max_tokens_per_rank. The intent is to correctly size dispatch buffers when sequence parallelism shards each DP rank's batch across EP ranks.

**Technical impact**  
DeepEPv2 now participates in the SP MoE path and its buffer sizing accounts for SP sharding, so each EP rank computes a ceil(n / sp_size) token bound instead of the full DP-rank batch size. This reduces the rounded-up power-of-two buffer size and avoids over-allocation or incorrect capacity assumptions during dispatch. The new sp_size parameter defaults to 1, preserving existing behavior for non-SP callers.

**Potential risks**  
The correctness depends on sp_size accurately reflecting the actual sharding of tokens across EP ranks; if the SP configuration and sp_size diverge, buffer sizing could be wrong and cause dispatch failures or incorrect results. The change is only wired through the DeepEPv2 branch of maybe_make_prepare_finalize, so other backends are unaffected, but the PR provides no test plan or results, leaving the SP path unverified. Edge cases such as sp_size greater than n or dp_metadata being None are handled by cdiv and the fallback, but remain untested.

**Key insights**  
The core fix is a one-line buffer-sizing correction (cdiv by sp_size) plus plumbing sp_size from the MoE parallel config into the DeepEPv2 prepare/finalize object. Because the default sp_size=1, the change is backward compatible for non-SP usage. The main gap is the absence of any test evidence for the newly enabled SP+DeepEPv2 combination, which is the highest-risk aspect of this otherwise small change.

  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/chat_completions/validate.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/openai/utils/types.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR introduces a shared reasoning-control resolution module (rust/src/chat/src/reasoning.rs) that normalizes how native Rust renderers (DeepSeek V3.2/V4/V4.1, Harmony, Inkling, Kimi K3) interpret thinking toggles and reasoning effort. It replaces the fixed ReasoningEffort enum with a flexible EffortValue (string or number), adds deployment-default inheritance via default_chat_template_kwargs, unifies precedence rules (thinking over enable_thinking, typed effort over kwargs effort), and makes effective_template_kwargs reflect the resolved prompt decision. The goal is cross-renderer consistency and parity with Python behavior.

**Technical impact**  
Renderers now take default_template_kwargs in their constructors and resolve reasoning through ReasoningControl::resolve with per-model fallback and validation. The old ReasoningEffort enum is removed from vllm_chat and re-added as an HTTP-only type in the server layer, with conversion to EffortValue at the boundary. New error variants (InvalidReasoningControl, InvalidReasoningEffort) map to HTTP 400 invalid_request_error. This changes public API signatures (renderer constructors, ChatOptions.reasoning_effort type) and behavior for effort handling, template kwargs export, and error classification.

**Potential risks**  
This is a broad, behavior-changing refactor across 29 files with altered public APIs, so downstream Rust consumers constructing renderers or using ReasoningEffort will break. Precedence and fallback semantics are subtle (e.g., explicit enable with effort 'none' inheriting deployment effort, standalone 'none' disabling), and regressions could silently change prompts or exported kwargs. Validation is CPU-only with no GPU generation or accuracy evaluation, so real-model output differences may be unverified. The new error mapping could reclassify previously template-level errors as request errors, affecting clients.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly separates source parsing, precedence resolution, and model-specific lowering, with model adapters owning supported effort names/ranges. Tests are extensive (785 passing, including roundtrips) and cover precedence, fallback, validation, and prompt/kwargs consistency. The main review focus should be on the precedence edge cases in ReasoningControl::from_kwargs/fallback and on ensuring the HTTP-to-EffortValue conversion preserves omission vs null vs 'none' semantics.

## 22. [[ROCm] triton+triton_kernels 3.8 mxfp4 MoE support (gpt-oss + DeepSeek-V4)](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55934)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #55934
- **Author:** [Rohan138](https://github.com/Rohan138)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 18:03:35
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55934/files) (9):**
  - `docker/Dockerfile.rock`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_gpt_oss_triton_kernels.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/config.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/experts/aiter_mxfp4_w4a16_moe.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/experts/aiter_mxfp4_w4a8_moe.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/experts/gpt_oss_triton_kernels_moe.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/oracle/mxfp4.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/utils/mxfp4_utils.py`
  - `vllm/utils/import_utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR adds support for triton_kernels 3.8's reorganized mxfp4 MoE API (matmul_ogs->matmul, RoutingData->RaggedTensorMetadata, weight_scale->b_mx_scale, removal of in-kernel scatter+topk-reduce) for gpt-oss and DeepSeek-V4 on ROCm. It introduces a shared get_triton_kernels_version dispatcher in import_utils.py that infers the API generation (3.5.1/3.6/3.8) by capability probing, and updates the gpt-oss MoE forward, mxfp4 oracle, config, and mxfp4_utils to branch on version. It also bumps the triton_kernels commit in Dockerfile.rock to ROCm releases/internal/3.8.x and forward-fixes the AITER_MXFP4_W4A8/W4A16 backends by removing the now-obsolete patch_gating_output workaround and routing weight-scale access through a new weight_mx_scale helper.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes across multiple MoE backends: the 3.8 path inlines a new RoutingData class and performs an external index_add combine since matmul no longer does in-kernel scatter+topk-reduce, and applies gfx950-specific opt-flag constraints (block_m=32, block_n=64, block_k=256) to avoid an autotuner padding cliff. The version dispatcher replaces the previous use_legacy_triton_kernels boolean and has_triton_kernels checks throughout, and PrecisionConfig construction now conditionally uses b_mx_scale/b_microblock_size versus weight_scale. The AITER W4A8/W4A16 backends drop the padding-mask gating patch and unwrap weight scales via weight_mx_scale, which is a forward-fix for an upcoming vLLM PR.

**Potential risks**  
The version detection is heuristic (probing for matmul_ogs and SparseMatrix imports) rather than reading a real version, so unusual or partially-installed triton_kernels builds could be misclassified. The new 3.8 routing path uses torch.argsort and scatter_add on the hot path, which may have performance or numerical implications versus the previous fused kernels, and the external index_add combine accumulates in float32 before casting. Benchmarks show aiter_triton_mxfp4_bf16 fails to boot under expert parallelism with a GPU memory access fault on both gfx942 and gfx950, and AITER_MXFP4_FP8 hits a quark loader IndexError under EP, indicating unresolved backend limitations. The Dockerfile change downgrades a hard build-time assertion into a warning, which could mask commit/tag mismatches between Docker and cmake builds.

**Key insights**  
The PR is a substantial multi-file change that adds a new triton_kernels 3.8 code path while preserving 3.5.1/3.6 compatibility, so it is not trivial. The capability-based version dispatcher is a pragmatic workaround for the package lacking a usable version string, but it couples vLLM to internal module layout. Benchmark data shows the new triton path is generally slower than the CK aiter path on gfx950, and the W4A16 aiter_triton backend is broken under EP, so the practical benefit is mainly enabling 3.8 compatibility rather than a performance win. Reviewers should focus on the correctness of the new external combine, the heuristic version detection, and the relaxed Dockerfile assertion.

## 23. [[ROCm][CI] Enable AITER FP8/unquantized cases in modular-kernel sweep + fix MoRI per-tensor FP8 dispatch](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57074)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57074
- **Author:** [divakar-amd](https://github.com/divakar-amd)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 17:53:06
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57074/files) (6):**
  - `.buildkite/test-amd.yaml`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/modular_kernel_tools/common.py`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/modular_kernel_tools/mk_objects.py`
  - `tests/kernels/moe/test_modular_kernel_combinations.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/all2all_utils.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/prepare_finalize/mori.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR enables AITER FP8 and fully-unquantized (None, None) cases in the modular-kernel MoE sweep by replacing hardcoded torch.float8_e4m3fn with current_platform.fp8_dtype()/is_fp8(), registering AiterExperts under common_float_types, and removing the module-level FNUZ skip so these cases finally run on MI300/gfx942. It also adds an explicit assert_aiter_quant_scheme_case() check and fixes a real MoRI bug where per-tensor FP8 configs fell through to unquantized dispatch while weights stayed FP8, causing a misleading 'Unsupported rhs dtype fp8e4b8' Triton assertion.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral changes span test harness, CI config, and production MoE dispatch code. all2all_utils.py now enables FP8 dispatch and sets scale_dim=1 for per-tensor quant, and mori.py adds a per-tensor quant branch that broadcasts the scalar scale to [num_tokens, 1] because MoRI reads scales via a raw pointer. The Buildkite AMD pipeline now runs test_modular_kernel_combinations.py separately with VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER=1 and VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER_MOE=1, and the AITER FP8 accuracy check is replaced with the shared check_accuracy helper at percent=0.9.

**Potential risks**  
The per-tensor MoRI fix touches production dispatch logic and relies on scale.expand(...).contiguous() to satisfy MoRI's raw-pointer scale layout; if the expected scale shape or dtype differs, this could silently mis-quantize or crash. The new AITER x DeepEP-HT/Mori skip is scoped to world_size>1 and references an unfixed pre-existing HIP illegal memory access (#57029), so real regressions in those combinations are masked until that issue is resolved. The is_valid() gate requiring AITER fused-moe to be enabled for MoRI may reduce coverage in environments where MoRI was previously exercised without AITER.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-scoped and clearly separates the harness enablement (step 1 of #54966) from the MXFP4/gfx950 follow-up, and it documents a pre-existing crash rather than hiding it. The MoRI per-tensor dispatch fix is the highest-value production change and is validated by a specific previously-failing test node. The switch to check_accuracy with percent=0.9 is a meaningful tolerance change from the prior custom 5% exceedance / 4x atol logic and should be confirmed to preserve intended FP8 precision expectations.

## 24. [[Bugfix][Metrics][MFU] Size activation traffic from the model dtype](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57070)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57070
- **Author:** [thillai-c](https://github.com/thillai-c)
- **Merged By:** [markmc](https://github.com/markmc)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 17:23:28
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57070/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/metrics/test_perf_metrics.py`
  - `vllm/v1/metrics/perf.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
In vllm/v1/metrics/perf.py, BaseConfigParser now sizes activation_byte_size from the resolved model torch_dtype via get_dtype_size instead of hardcoding 2 bytes, matching how weight_byte_size is already computed. This corrects MFU/MBU accounting for float32 models, where activation traffic was previously understated by half. Tests were added to verify activation byte size follows dtype and that attention read bytes scale accordingly.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes only for non-2-byte dtypes: float16 and bfloat16 remain at 2 bytes, while float32 activation read/write traffic doubles and reported MBU drops correspondingly. This is analytic metrics accounting only, with no effect on kernels, model computation, or logits. Quantization parsers that override weight_byte_size but not activation_byte_size remain consistent since activations follow the model compute dtype.

**Potential risks**  
The float32 MBU change is user-visible and may surprise users tracking metrics, though it is a correction rather than a regression. The assumption that activations always use the model compute dtype could be inaccurate for some quantized or mixed-precision setups where activation storage differs from the model dtype, potentially introducing new inaccuracies. Test coverage relies on mock configs and may not capture all real-world dtype/quantization combinations.

**Key insights**  
The fix removes a FIXME by keying activation sizing on the same resolved dtype already used for weights, eliminating an internal inconsistency. The added tests are well-targeted, including an end-to-end check through AttentionMetrics.get_read_bytes_breakdown that qkv_input doubles for float32 versus bfloat16. The change is small and low-risk for the dominant bf16/fp16 deployments, with impact concentrated on float32 users.

## 25. [[Bugfix][Spec Decode] Fix EAGLE and dense draft startup with EP](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56930)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56930
- **Author:** [kyleliang-nv](https://github.com/kyleliang-nv)
- **Merged By:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 17:03:37
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56930/files) (2):**
  - `tests/test_config.py`
  - `vllm/config/speculative.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a regression from #55914 where an EP-enabled MoE target paired with a dense EAGLE/EAGLE3/independent draft failed config validation with 'Number of experts in the model must be greater than 0 when expert parallelism is enabled.' The fix passes the final draft ModelConfig (after architecture overrides) into create_draft_parallel_config and disables inherited EP only when ModelConfig.is_moe reports a dense draft, keeping EP inheritance for MoE drafts like DeepSeek V4 MTP and DSpark.

**Technical impact**  
create_draft_parallel_config gains an optional draft_model_config argument; when provided, enable_expert_parallel becomes target_ep AND draft_model_config.is_moe, aligning EP with the same is_moe classification used by the existing expert-count validator. The production call site now supplies the final draft config, while two-argument or explicit-None calls retain prior EP inheritance for backward compatibility. Target EP and target/draft TP settings are unchanged.

**Potential risks**  
The optional argument means callers that omit draft_model_config still inherit EP and can hit the original validation failure, so the fix is incomplete for any out-of-tree or future call sites that don't pass the config. Correctness depends on ModelConfig.is_moe accurately classifying drafts, including after architecture overrides; a misclassification could wrongly enable or disable EP. Tests cover configuration construction and validation only, with no inference or accuracy evaluation, so runtime behavior of the draft worker under the new EP setting is unverified.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between EP inheritance and the is_moe-based expert-count validator, resolved by reusing the same classification rather than inferring density. The PR includes a negative control showing that omitting the draft config at the production call site causes 6 dense-draft/target-EP validation failures, confirming the tests catch a missing call-site update. It overlaps with #56933 and adopts the same backward-compatible optional-model-config interface, so maintainers may consolidate the two fixes.

## 26. [[ROCm][CI] Shard MI300 Multimodal Processor](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57056)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57056
- **Author:** [aarushjain29](https://github.com/aarushjain29)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 16:55:14
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57056/files) (1):**
  - `.buildkite/test-amd.yaml`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The MI300 Multimodal Processor CI step in .buildkite/test-amd.yaml was sharded into 4 parallel jobs by adding parallelism: 4 and passing --num-shards/--shard-id to pytest, and the label was updated to include Shard %N. This was done because the unsharded step took 101.9 min against a 110 min timeout (93% of budget), risking timeouts as models are added.

**Technical impact**  
Wall-clock time for the slowest shard drops to roughly 34.5 min (about 2.95x speedup), reducing timeout utilization from 93% to 57% of the 110 min budget. Aggregate GPU time increases from 101.9 to 128.6 min because the ~8.9 min setup is now paid per shard. No test code, selection, or coverage changes; the 160 tests are independent and already process-isolated.

**Potential risks**  
The timeout_in_minutes remains 110 while the PR description references a 60 min target, so the timeout may not have been tightened as intended. Sharding relies on BUILDKITE_PARALLEL_JOB_COUNT and BUILDKITE_PARALLEL_JOB being set correctly by Buildkite; misconfiguration could cause uneven shards or missing tests. Increased aggregate GPU usage may affect shared MI300 pool capacity.

**Key insights**  
This is a low-risk CI configuration change that follows the existing sharding pattern used by other steps in the same file. The main tradeoff is reduced wall-clock latency at the cost of higher total GPU time due to per-shard setup overhead. The label comment still says TBD, and the timeout value appears inconsistent with the stated 60 min target, which is worth verifying.

## 28. [[Frontend] Add per-request metrics to Responses API](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55084)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #55084
- **Author:** [xinnywinne](https://github.com/xinnywinne)
- **Merged By:** [benchislett](https://github.com/benchislett)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 15:52:06
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55084/files) (6):**
  - `docs/features/per_request_metrics.md`
  - `tests/entrypoints/openai/responses/test_serving_responses.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/generate/api_router.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/openai/responses/context.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/openai/responses/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/openai/responses/serving.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds per-request timing metrics to the /v1/responses endpoint, reusing PerRequestMetrics and build_per_request_timing_metrics from the existing Chat Completions/Completions implementation. Metrics are included in non-streaming responses and only in the terminal response.completed event for streaming, gated by the --enable-per-request-metrics server flag, and suppressed for multi-turn built-in-tool flows. Documentation and tests were added alongside the implementation.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a new optional metrics field on ResponsesResponse (excluded when None to preserve existing response shape), threads enable_per_request_metrics through the generate API router and OpenAIServingResponses constructor, and adds request_metrics plus request_metrics_cover_all_generation_turns tracking to ConversationContext. The streaming path relies on the context's stored metrics being populated before the completed event is emitted, and the multi-turn suppression flag is set when built-in tools trigger additional model generations.

**Potential risks**  
The suppression heuristic for built-in-tool flows is a coarse boolean flag; if a future flow has multiple generations but still wants metrics, it would be silently omitted. Streaming correctness depends on context.request_metrics being set from the last RequestOutput before the completed event, which could be fragile if the generator ordering changes. The new field is added to a widely-used response model, so any downstream consumer assuming a fixed schema could be affected, though exclude_if mitigates this when disabled.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped and reuses existing metric-building infrastructure rather than duplicating logic, and the tests cover the flag on/off, streaming vs non-streaming, and multi-turn suppression cases. The explicit request_metrics_cover_all_generation_turns flag documents the known limitation that engine timestamps only cover one turn while usage is aggregated, which is a reasonable tradeoff. The PR description notes AI assistance and includes E2E validation on A100, which increases confidence, but reviewers should verify the streaming event ordering assumption and the multi-turn suppression semantics.

## 29. [[Frontend] Return prompt metadata from /inference/v1/generate](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53187)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #53187
- **Author:** [aoshen02](https://github.com/aoshen02)
- **Merged By:** [ywang96](https://github.com/ywang96)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 15:29:08
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53187/files) (9):**
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/inference/generate.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/inference/generate/convert.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/inference/generate/types.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/routes/render.rs`
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/test_generate_stream.py`
  - `tests/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/test_serving_multimodal_tokens.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/mm_features.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/scale_out/token_in_token_out/serving.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Implements phase 2 of RFC #51472 by exposing prompt metadata from /inference/v1/generate when return_token_ids=true. Both Python and Rust frontends now return prompt_token_ids (final prompt after multimodal placeholder expansion) and mm_placeholders (per-modality offset/length ranges), with streaming responses carrying them once on the first emitted data chunk. The placeholder extraction logic from /render's extract_mm_features() was factored into a shared placeholder_ranges_from_engine_input() helper to avoid serializing full pixel_values.

**Technical impact**  
Adds new optional fields to GenerateRequest, GenerateResponse, and GenerateStreamResponse in both frontends, keeping the Python/Rust contract identical. ResponseOptions in Rust now carries return_token_ids, and the streaming path tracks prompt_token_ids and mm_placeholders to emit them exactly once. Behavior is unchanged when the flag is omitted or false, and encoder-decoder models omit mm_placeholders since offsets refer to the decoder prompt. A zero-token completion still emits its terminal chunk when metadata is pending so prompt_token_ids is never lost.

**Potential risks**  
Streaming parity between Python and Rust relies on Python now excluding None keys to match Rust's skip_serializing_none; any future field additions must maintain this symmetry. The zero-token completion change alters when an empty terminal chunk is emitted, which could affect downstream consumers that assumed empty deltas are always skipped. The mm_placeholders field is gated on return_token_ids, so callers wanting only placeholder ranges must also request token IDs. The model-backed test requires a collapse step because /generate re-expands placeholders, which is a known separate issue (RFC #51472 Problem 2) and could confuse integrators.

**Key insights**  
The PR is well-scoped and reuses existing infrastructure (extract_mm_features, skip_serializing_none) rather than duplicating logic. Test coverage is thorough across Python unit, Rust unit, and model-backed tests, including edge cases like n=2 with empty first output, zero-token completions, and encoder-decoder omission. The design decision to gate mm_placeholders on return_token_ids is justified since offsets are meaningless without the token IDs. The independent audit findings (flag-off extraction cost, stream key parity, zero-token completions) appear addressed in the diff.
