## 30. [[BugFix][KV Connector] Fix Deadlock with KVConnector + MTP under KV Pressure](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57104)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57104
- **Author:** [robertgshaw2-redhat](https://github.com/robertgshaw2-redhat)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 15:22:08
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57104/files) (3):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_remote_prefill_lifecycle.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/utils.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a scheduler deadlock that occurs under KV memory pressure when using a KV connector with async remote prefill plus MTP/speculative decoding. The admission gate for async loads previously reserved only the blocks needed to hold the full sequence, ignoring the extra lookahead/spec-decode step blocks that promotion later requires, so two loads could be admitted that together exhausted the pool and the head request could never be promoted. The fix adds a _spec_decode_step_blocks() helper and includes it in both the async-load admission reservation and _request_remaining_blocks().

**Technical impact**  
Changes scheduler admission accounting so async KV loads reserve blocks for the full sequence plus the spec decode step margin (cdiv(1 + num_spec_tokens + num_lookahead_tokens, block_size)). This makes admission more conservative when speculative tokens are enabled, preventing the head-of-queue request from being stuck failing allocate_slots while nothing is running to free blocks. Behavior is unchanged when num_spec_tokens is zero, since the helper returns 0.

**Potential risks**  
The added reservation is conservative and may reduce effective concurrency or delay admission of async loads under tight KV budgets, potentially lowering throughput in spec-decode plus connector workloads. Correctness depends on the margin formula matching the actual blocks allocate_slots requests at promotion time; if lookahead or spec token accounting diverges, the deadlock could reappear or over-reservation could cause unnecessary stalls. The change touches core scheduler admission logic, so regressions would affect all KV-connector async prefill paths, not just MTP.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a mismatch between blocks reserved at admission and blocks actually needed to start running after the async load completes, which is a classic reservation-vs-consumption bug that manifests as a livelock rather than a crash. The fix is well-targeted and covered by a new unit test that reproduces the exact 4+3 block scenario with 7 usable blocks, plus a test utility extension to configure speculative tokens. Reviewers should verify the margin formula against the real allocate_slots lookahead behavior and confirm no meaningful throughput regression for non-spec-decode connector users.

---

## 31. [[Bugfix] Add Responses cache_write_tokens for API compat and CC parity](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57222)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57222
- **Author:** [yzong-rh](https://github.com/yzong-rh)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 14:52:35
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57222/files) (5):**
  - `tests/entrypoints/openai/responses/test_serving_responses.py`
  - `tests/entrypoints/unit_tests/test_context.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/openai/responses/context.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/openai/responses/protocol.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/openai/responses/serving.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a cache_write_tokens field to the Responses API InputTokensDetails protocol and populates it from a new num_cache_creation_tokens counter tracked in SimpleContext. This exposes prompt-cache write usage through the official usage.input_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens field, aligning Responses with Chat Completions semantics and making the response usage valid against OpenAI's ResponseUsage model. Tests were updated to cover the new field and validate against the OpenAI model.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the Responses API usage payload shape by adding a required cache_write_tokens integer to InputTokensDetails, and adds a num_cache_creation_tokens attribute to SimpleContext that is updated from RequestOutput.num_cache_creation_tokens when present. serving.py uses getattr with a default of 0, so ParsableContext and HarmonyContext remain unaffected. The new field is required (no default) in the Pydantic model, so any other code constructing InputTokensDetails must supply it.

**Potential risks**  
Making cache_write_tokens a required field without a default could break other call sites constructing InputTokensDetails if any exist outside the updated serving path. The getattr fallback masks missing attributes but could silently report 0 for contexts that do not track cache creation. The counter is only updated when num_cache_creation_tokens is not None, so a None value after a prior non-None value would retain a stale count, though this is unlikely in practice. Behavior is scoped to SimpleContext, so ParsableContext/HarmonyContext users see no change.

**Key insights**  
The change is small but behavior-affecting: it adds a new API field and a new context counter, plus test coverage validating OpenAI ResponseUsage compatibility. The PR description notes the zero-write case was exercised end-to-end and a longer prompt was used for write propagation, but the pasted results only show cache_write_tokens=0, so the non-zero write path is not directly evidenced in the provided output. Overall the diff is coherent and low-risk given the getattr fallback and scoped context changes.

## 32. [[Build] Bump DeepGEMM pin to a6bbb80](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57218)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57218
- **Author:** [zyongye](https://github.com/zyongye)
- **Merged By:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 14:49:36
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57218/files) (2):**
  - `cmake/external_projects/deepgemm.cmake`
  - `tools/install_deepgemm.sh`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Bumps the vllm-project/DeepGEMM fork pin from ad1f172 to a6bbb800 (fork dev head) in both cmake/external_projects/deepgemm.cmake and tools/install_deepgemm.sh, which are documented as needing to stay in sync. The bump picks up three upstream fork fixes: SM120 GEMM/MQA path corrections plus test reorganization (#9), removal of the Mega-Gate finite-score device trap while retaining the expert-ID clamp (#11), and an increase of kNumTimeoutCycles from 60s to 300s (#13).

**Technical impact**  
Changes the fetched dependency revision for all DeepGEMM builds, affecting the compiled _C extension across architectures since the modified csrc/apis headers are compiled unconditionally. The timeout change lengthens the shared device-side spin-wait budget used by the NVLink cross-rank barrier, intra-device grid sync, and mega-MoE combine peer wait, reducing spurious device asserts on slow-but-progressing multi-rank workloads. The Mega-Gate change alters failure behavior (non-finite scores now propagate rather than aborting on device) rather than numerics on well-formed inputs.

**Potential risks**  
The new ref must remain fetchable and its declared submodules (third-party/cutlass, third-party/deep_jit) must resolve; a force-push or branch rewrite on the fork's dev branch would break reproducibility. The 300s timeout could mask genuine hangs for longer before failing, and the removal of the device trap may allow non-finite scores to propagate into downstream computation. SM120 correctness fixes in #9 are unverified on the author's SM103 hardware, and there is a potential trivial textual conflict with open PR #56266 which also touches deepgemm.cmake.

**Key insights**  
The change is a dependency pin bump with no vLLM code path modifications, but it is classified SUBSTANTIAL because it alters build inputs and runtime failure/timeout behavior. Verification was limited to confirming the ref resolves, submodules fetch, and _C compiles; no model evaluation was performed, and the author explicitly notes SM120 hardware is unavailable for validating #9. Reviewers should weigh whether an SM120 or Mega-MoE accuracy run is warranted before merge.

## 33. [[CI][Bugfix] Initialize _transfer_layer_group_ids in region_pull_worker fixture](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57211)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57211
- **Author:** [vllm-agent](https://github.com/vllm-agent)
- **Merged By:** [robertgshaw2-redhat](https://github.com/robertgshaw2-redhat)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 14:20:29
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57211/files) (1):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_nixl_connector_hma.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR fixes a CI failure on main where test_region_pull_ignores_allocation_padding failed with AttributeError because the region_pull_worker fixture constructs NixlConnectorWorker via object.__new__ and manually sets attributes, but omitted _transfer_layer_group_ids. A stale-base merge conflict between PR #50494 (which added the attribute and reads it in base_worker.py) and PR #57077 (which added the fixture) caused the breakage. The fix adds worker._transfer_layer_group_ids = () to the fixture, matching the base-class default and the sibling _make_mock_worker_for_desc_ids fixture.

**Technical impact**  
Restores the test suite to green by ensuring the manually-constructed worker has the attribute that base_worker.py reads via bool(self._transfer_layer_group_ids). The empty tuple default preserves the intended behavior of disabling layer-name routing, so no production code paths are affected. This is a test-only change with no runtime impact on the NIXL connector itself.

**Potential risks**  
Minimal risk since the change is confined to a test fixture and mirrors an existing pattern. The main residual risk is that other manually-constructed worker fixtures may have similar missing attributes that could surface later, and the author notes the fix was not validated locally (no torch), so correctness relies on CI. If the base class default ever changes, this hardcoded value could drift out of sync.

**Key insights**  
This is a classic stale-base merge conflict where two independently-green PRs break main when combined, highlighting the value of rebasing or merge-queue validation before landing. The fixture pattern of object.__new__ plus manual attribute assignment is fragile because it bypasses __init__ and silently misses new attributes added to the base class. A more robust approach would be to centralize fixture construction or assert required attributes to catch such drift earlier.

## 34. [[Refactor] Remove unused interface methods](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57194)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57194
- **Author:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged By:** [sfeng33](https://github.com/sfeng33)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 14:00:49

  - `tests/entrypoints/unit_tests/test_context.py`
  - `tests/tool_parsers/test_structural_tag_registry.py`
  - `vllm/parser/abstract_parser.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR trims the Parser base class in vllm/parser/abstract_parser.py to only the members actually consumed through the Parser type, removing unused abstract methods (extract_content_ids, extract_reasoning, extract_reasoning_streaming, extract_tool_calls, extract_tool_calls_streaming, find_reasoning_end_offset, is_reasoning_end_streaming), the vocab cached_property, and the reasoning_parser/tool_parser setters. DelegatingParser's internal helpers are renamed to private (_is_reasoning_end_streaming, _extract_content_ids) and tests are updated to use the private _reasoning_parser/_tool_parser attributes. The goal is to reduce the interface surface to what the serving layer actually calls.

**Technical impact**  
This is a public API/interface change: any external subclass or caller relying on the removed abstract methods, the vocab property, or the public setters will break. Internally, DelegatingParser still composes the legacy parsers but now exposes fewer members, and ParserEngine no longer inherits methods it never used. Tests were adjusted to match the new private attribute names, indicating the change is behavior-preserving for the serving path but not for the type contract.

**Potential risks**  
Removing abstract methods and setters is a breaking change for downstream code, plugins, or third-party parsers that subclass Parser or set reasoning_parser/tool_parser directly. The rename of is_reasoning_end_streaming and extract_content_ids to private methods could silently break any external callers. The diff is truncated, so the full extent of changes in abstract_parser.py and any remaining references to removed members cannot be fully verified from the provided information.

**Key insights**  
The refactor is a deliberate interface narrowing rather than a pure cleanup, so it should be treated as a breaking API change requiring a deprecation or migration note. The test updates (switching to _reasoning_parser/_tool_parser) confirm the private attribute convention is now the intended access path. Reviewers should verify no remaining references to the removed public methods exist elsewhere in the codebase and consider whether a changelog entry is warranted.

## 35. [[BugFix] Fix is_supported of cutlass FP8 linear (selected and fails on A100)](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55884)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #55884
- **Author:** [danisereb](https://github.com/danisereb)
- **Merged By:** [vllm-bot](https://github.com/vllm-bot)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 12:31:43
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55884/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/scaled_mm/cutlass.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR fixes a bug where CutlassFP8ScaledMMLinearKernel.is_supported incorrectly returned True on non-CUDA and unsupported CUDA architectures like A100 (sm80). It replaces the simple current_platform.is_cuda() check with cutlass_fp8_supported(), which properly validates CUTLASS FP8 kernel availability, allowing vLLM to fall back to MarlinFP8ScaledMMLinearKernel on A100.

**Technical impact**  
This changes backend selection logic for FP8 linear layers, ensuring that on Ampere/A100 the unsupported CUTLASS FP8 kernel is not selected and a valid alternative (Marlin) is used. It also broadens the support check beyond just CUDA to account for actual CUTLASS FP8 capability, which may affect other platforms or compute capabilities.

**Potential risks**  
The behavior now depends on the correctness of cutlass_fp8_supported(); if that function has edge cases or is not properly maintained, it could incorrectly enable or disable the CUTLASS kernel. There is also a risk that on some platforms where CUTLASS FP8 is supported but cutlass_fp8_supported() returns False, performance may regress due to fallback to a slower kernel. The change is small and localized, but it alters runtime backend selection.

**Key insights**  
The fix correctly addresses a platform-specific bug by using a more precise capability check instead of a generic CUDA check. It highlights the importance of aligning kernel support predicates with actual hardware/software support. The test plan (GSM8K on A100 with Nemotron-3) validates the fix end-to-end, though broader regression testing across other architectures would strengthen confidence.

## 36. [[Perf][DSV4.1] Remove MegaMoE padding and shared padding workaround](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57204)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57204
- **Author:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged By:** [WoosukKwon](https://github.com/WoosukKwon)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 12:09:30
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57204/files) (2):**
  - `tests/models/test_deepseek_v4_mega_moe.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v4/nvidia/model.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Removes the MegaMoE intermediate-width padding (2304 to 2560) for DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash and reverts the shared-expert padding workaround from #56568. Routed and shared experts now retain the native checkpoint width of 2304, keeping native shared-expert fusion enabled without padding, since the bundled DeepGEMM no longer requires 16-byte TMA-row alignment for activation-scale rows. Tests were rewritten to assert exact checkpoint-shaped weight/scale preservation, native-width shared fusion, buffer reuse, and repeated finalization.

**Technical impact**  
Eliminates 10% of the intermediate dimension used by padded expert GEMMs and removes separate padded shared-weight copies, reducing memory and compute. The runtime divisibility-by-128 check is retained. Shared-expert fusion eligibility checks and disable flag are unchanged. The change touches both the model implementation and its regression tests, altering weight-loading and finalization behavior for the V4.1-Flash path.

**Potential risks**  
Removing padding assumes the bundled DeepGEMM's layout::Data(..., false) path fully removes the TMA-row alignment requirement; any environment using an older or different DeepGEMM could regress. The change also removes the fallback path where non-fused shared experts kept checkpoint-shaped weights, so any code relying on that behavior may break. Validation was performed on GB200 with a specific upstream commit, so other hardware or configurations are less covered.

**Key insights**  
The PR correctly couples the removal of routed padding with the revert of the shared-padding workaround, since a bare revert of #56568 would leave routed padding in place and disable V4.1 shared fusion. Test coverage was preserved and adapted rather than dropped. Reported results show 30 tests passing and 48/48 zero-error operator comparisons against upstream, though the GSM8K numbers are prior model-path validations rather than a new isolated comparison.

## 37. [[ROCm][Bugfix] Revert #56433 + #51692 to fix accuracy breakdown for DeepSeek-V4](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57132)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57132
- **Author:** [shen-shanshan](https://github.com/shen-shanshan)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 11:58:24
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57132/files) (8):**
  - `tests/models/test_deepseek_v4_vl_rocm.py`
  - `vllm/_aiter_ops.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/scaled_mm/aiter.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/attention/mla_attention.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/linear.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v4/amd/model.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v4/amd/rocm.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR reverts two prior changes (#56433 and #51692) that introduced the AiterPreshuffledFp8BlockScaledMMKernel and related weight-preshuffling logic for DeepSeek-V4 on ROCm. The revert was necessary because the new kernel path caused gsm8k accuracy to collapse to ~0 on AMD GPUs, and reverting only #56433 broke server launch with a NotImplementedError. The PR removes the preshuffled kernel class, its registration, the is_blockscale_bpreshuffle_tuned helper, and the weight_already_preshuffled guards, restoring the previous hand-shuffle behavior.

**Technical impact**  
Removes AiterPreshuffledFp8BlockScaledMMKernel from the linear kernel registry and ROCm platform kernel list, deletes its implementation (~170 lines), and simplifies _load_gemm_tuned_configs back to a fixed q_dtype_w filter. The MLA attention now uses _cpu_skip_gemm_dispatch instead of skip_weight_relayout, and DeepSeek-V4 AMD model code unconditionally shuffles weights rather than checking if the kernel already did so. The fused norm+quant path in rocm.py reverts to a simpler boolean check and always uses transpose_scale=False.

**Potential risks**  
The revert removes the preshuffled FP8 block-scaled kernel entirely, which may reduce performance for configurations that benefited from it, though accuracy is restored. The change to _load_gemm_tuned_configs drops the generic filters/key_cols API, so any future callers needing different filters would need to re-add that flexibility. The removal of weight_already_preshuffled means the hand-shuffle code now assumes no kernel-level preshuffling, which is consistent with the reverted state but could conflict if the kernel is reintroduced later without updating these call sites. The test file test_deepseek_v4_vl_rocm.py loses 125 lines of tests covering the removed functionality, reducing coverage for the reverted code paths.

**Key insights**  
The root cause appears to be a mismatch between the activation-scale byte order expected by the bpreshuffle GEMM and what the fused norm+quant path produced, leading to numerically wrong results for M > 1. The revert is a pragmatic fix that trades potential performance gains for correctness, and the PR description provides clear before/after accuracy evidence (0.0053 to 0.9538 on gsm8k). The interdependency between #56433 and #51692 means both had to be reverted together to avoid the NotImplementedError, highlighting the coupling risk in the original changes.

---

## 38. [[MM] Keep raw pixels through dp-sharded ViT path](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56872)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56872
- **Author:** [cjackal](https://github.com/cjackal)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 11:47:28
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56872/files) (3):**
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/qwen2_5_vl.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/qwen2_vl.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/vision.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR removes the pre-cast of pixel values to the vision model dtype before calling run_dp_sharded_mrope_vision_model in qwen2_vl.py and qwen2_5_vl.py, and instead derives the placeholder embedding dtype from the vision model's own parameter dtype inside run_dp_sharded_mrope_vision_model. This is a follow-up to #55370, where the pre-cast was introduced because all-gathered placeholders forced the input dtype to match pixel_values.dtype, which broke when some encoder DP ranks received no images.

**Technical impact**  
The placeholder tensors created for empty-image ranks now use the vision model's parameter dtype rather than the raw pixel_values dtype, decoupling the input pixel dtype from the output embedding dtype. This allows raw (e.g., lower-precision or different) pixel values to flow through the DP-sharded ViT path without an explicit cast, while keeping the all-gather dtype consistent across ranks.

**Potential risks**  
If the vision model's parameter dtype differs from the dtype expected by downstream consumers of the embeddings, or if mixed dtypes are used across ranks, there could be subtle dtype mismatches. The change relies on next(vision_model.parameters()).dtype being representative of the model's compute/output dtype, which may not hold for models with mixed parameter dtypes or non-standard parameter ordering. Test coverage appears limited to a single Qwen2-VL scenario with TP>=2 and one image.

**Key insights**  
The fix correctly addresses the root cause by making the placeholder dtype follow the model output dtype instead of the input pixel dtype, which is the semantically correct choice for an output buffer. Removing the pre-cast simplifies the call sites and avoids unnecessary dtype conversions, but reviewers should verify that all DP ranks produce consistent embedding dtypes and that downstream code tolerates the raw pixel dtype input.

## 40. [[Rust Frontend] Expose local DP size in gRPC Control metadata](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57116)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57116
- **Author:** [alec-flowers](https://github.com/alec-flowers)
- **Merged By:** [njhill](https://github.com/njhill)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 11:28:09
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57116/files) (3):**
  - `rust/proto/control.proto`
  - `rust/src/server/src/grpc/control.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/grpc/tests.rs`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new proto field ParallelismInfo.data_parallel_size_local (tag 7) and populates it in the Rust gRPC Control service from EngineCoreClient::engine_count(), exposing how many DP ranks a given frontend locally serves. This supports hybrid load balancing for Dynamo's native gRPC sidecar integration, where sidecars need the local rank count to register rank ranges and normalize aggregate KV capacity. The test suite was refactored to cover single-engine, complete-group, and partial-group (global DP8 with start ranks 0 and 4) scenarios.

**Technical impact**  
Extends the Control gRPC schema with a backward-compatible additive field; older servers omit it and consumers must treat zero as unknown rather than an empty rank range. The producer reads engine_count() after startup has waited for the configured engine set, so successfully started frontends always report a positive count. Tests were rewritten to use the bootstrapped transport and connect_to_bootstrapped_frontend helper, exercising multi-engine aggregation across varying global sizes and start ranks.

**Potential risks**  
Consumers that misinterpret zero as an empty rank range could mis-register ownership; the PR explicitly requires rejecting nonzero-start ownership without a local count and only allowing the legacy complete-group fallback at starting rank zero. The cast of engine_count() to u32 is safe in practice but unchecked. The benchmark validation used a patched nightly build rather than this exact commit, so end-to-end verification against this upstream revision is not fully demonstrated.

**Key insights**  
The change is a small, additive schema/producer update whose correctness depends heavily on downstream consumers honoring the zero-means-unknown contract. Test coverage meaningfully improved by parameterizing over start rank and global DP size, confirming local capacity stays scoped to attached engines. No model accuracy impact since only discovery metadata is exposed.

## 41. [[Bugfix] Fix np.float64 leaking into the KV transfer metrics log](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57068)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57068
- **Author:** [NickLucche](https://github.com/NickLucche)
- **Merged By:** [NickLucche](https://github.com/NickLucche)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 11:09:06
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57068/files) (7):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_hf3fs_connector.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_mooncake_stats.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_multi_connector.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_nixl_connector.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/hf3fs/hf3fs_connector.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/mooncake/stats.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/stats.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR fixes a bug where numpy scalar types (e.g. np.float64) leaked into the KV transfer metrics log when stats were aggregated through a MultiConnector. Because MultiConnector's reduce() returns a nested dict rendered with str(), numpy 2's repr produced 'np.float64(2.338)' instead of plain numbers. The fix calls .item() on numpy scalars in the nixl, mooncake, and hf3fs reduce() implementations so they return plain Python int/float values, matching their existing dict[str, int \\| float] type annotations.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior of the metrics reduce() methods changes to return native Python scalars rather than numpy scalars, which is what the type annotations already promised. This makes CLI log output clean and consistent across single-connector and MultiConnector paths, and avoids downstream code that assumes plain Python numbers from these dicts. Four test files were updated to assert no np.generic values remain and to verify the rendered log line contains plain numbers.

**Potential risks**  
The change is low risk but relies on .item() being valid on the numpy results; if any of these expressions could return a non-numpy value or an array of size >1, .item() would raise. The throughput_mb_s expression in mooncake applies .item() only in the non-zero branch, which is correct, but any future refactor must preserve that. Tests cover the main connectors but not every edge case (e.g. empty stats paths) for the scalar-type guarantee.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is the subtle difference between str() and repr() formatting of numpy scalars in nested dicts, which only surfaced in the MultiConnector aggregation path. Enforcing plain Python scalars at the reduce() boundary is the right fix since it aligns implementation with the declared return type and prevents similar repr leakage elsewhere. Adding explicit np.generic assertions to tests is a good regression guard for this class of bug.

## 42. [[Bugfix][Model] Restore causal image SWA for DeepSeek V4.1](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57152)

- **Author:** [Juntian777](https://github.com/Juntian777)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 11:09:01
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57152/files) (8):**
  - `tests/v1/attention/test_deepseek_v4_swa_visible.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/attention.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/common/ops/cache_utils.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/flash_mla_mega_attn.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/flashinfer_sparse.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/flashmla.py`
  - `vllm/transformers_utils/configs/deepseek_v41.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/mla/sparse_swa.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR restores causal sliding-window attention (SWA) for image tokens in DeepSeek V4.1 VL, which had incorrectly inherited bidirectional image visibility from V4 Vision-Exp. It removes the multimodal prefix-LM configuration, strips the image-window widening logic from the shared SWA metadata builder and Triton kernels, sets the V4.1 attention-side image window extension to zero, and adds six regression tests verifying causal key positions in gathered and paged indices.

**Technical impact**  
The change removes left_visible/right_visible/max_image_tokens parameters from combine_topk_swa_indices, build_flashinfer_mixed_sparse_indices, and related kernels, simplifying the SWA index construction to a plain causal window. Workspace allocation (combined_topk) shrinks by max_image_tokens, and the V4.1 config no longer sets is_mm_prefix_lm or mm_prefix_clamp_sliding_window. V4 Vision-Exp behavior is preserved via the mm_prefix_clamp_sliding_window gate in sparse_swa.py.

**Potential risks**  
Full-model VL accuracy, generation-length evaluation, and AMD runtime validation were not run, so end-to-end correctness is unverified beyond metadata/kernel-level tests. The three existing builder tests were deselected due to an inaccessible gated Hugging Face config, reducing coverage. Any other model or code path that relied on the removed left_visible/right_visible parameters or the is_mm_prefix_lm config attribute could break if not updated.

**Key insights**  
The fix aligns V4.1 with the official reference implementation where get_window_topk_idxs masks indices greater than the query position, ensuring image and text tokens share the same causal 128-token window. The regression tests validate both full prefill and continuation chunks across compression ratios 0/1/2, checking gather/CPU-plan consistency and absence of image visibility extensions. The change is well-scoped and the diff is net-negative in lines, indicating removal of complexity rather than addition.

---

  - `vllm/v1/worker/startup_plan.py`
  - `vllm/v1/worker/worker_base.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR fixes a CI failure on main caused by ruff's D209 (new-line-after-last-paragraph) rule, which was flagging docstrings introduced alongside PR #52136. It adds D209 to the ignore list in pyproject.toml, reverts the docstring reformatting that D209 had caused across 692 files (rejoining 2085 closing triple-quotes onto their last content line), and explicitly ignores D203 and D213 to silence ruff's incompatible-rule warnings while pinning the same resolution ruff already chose. The change is scoped to only the docstrings that #52136 split, leaving the ~6900 pre-existing split docstrings untouched.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior is unchanged: the sweep is whitespace-only, verified by byte-identical inspect.cleandoc() output across all 692 files, stable ruff check/ruff format, and successful py_compile. The pyproject.toml change stops ruff from enforcing D209 going forward and removes two warning lines from every ruff invocation, making ruff check --statistics output byte-identical before and after. No runtime, model, or API behavior is affected.

**Potential risks**  
Nothing enforces the joined docstring style going forward since ruff has no inverse of D209, so new code can drift back to the split style without CI catching it. The sweep is script-generated and touches 693 files, so a scoping error could have reformatted unrelated docstrings, though the author verified the scope against #52136's diff and confirmed content equivalence. The explicit D203/D213 ignores pin ruff's current resolution, which could become stale if ruff changes its default conflict handling.

**Key insights**  
This is a well-scoped revert-plus-config change rather than a restyle: matching against #52136's own diff limited the sweep to 692 files instead of 8972 across 2484 files. The author correctly identified that ignoring D205 (summary/body separator) while enforcing D209 (closing-quote placement) was an inconsistent pair of choices. The verification methodology (cleandoc byte-equality, ruff stability, py_compile) is thorough for a whitespace-only change, and the explicit D203/D213 ignores are a reasonable way to make ruff's implicit conflict resolution explicit and warning-free.

## 44. [[Benchmark] Retire stale benchmarks and consolidate RMSNorm](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57083)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57083
- **Author:** [mgoin](https://github.com/mgoin)
- **Merged By:** [mgoin](https://github.com/mgoin)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 10:21:44
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57083/files) (7):**
  - `benchmarks/cutlass_benchmarks/utils.py`
  - `benchmarks/cutlass_benchmarks/w8a8_benchmarks.py`
  - `benchmarks/cutlass_benchmarks/weight_shapes.py`
  - `benchmarks/kernels/benchmark_rmsnorm.py`
  - `benchmarks/kernels/ir/shapes.py`
  - `benchmarks/overheads/benchmark_hashing.py`
  - `tools/pre_commit/check_forbidden_imports.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR removes the legacy CUTLASS benchmark directory (utils.py, w8a8_benchmarks.py, weight_shapes.py), the standalone RMSNorm benchmark script, and the hashing profiler that referenced the removed hash_of_block function. It also consolidates RMSNorm benchmarking into the IR harness by generating shape configs for both rms_norm and fused_add_rms_norm, and cleans up stale pickle-import exceptions in the forbidden-imports pre-commit check. The net effect is 3 added and 780 removed lines, reducing maintenance burden and eliminating dead code.

**Technical impact**  
Benchmark coverage for RMSNorm is preserved through the shared IR shape grid, with --ops rms_norm now selecting both plain and residual variants. The forbidden-imports lint no longer needs exceptions for the deleted CUTLASS scripts, and the hashing profiler that would have failed at runtime is gone. No production code paths are touched, so runtime behavior of vLLM itself is unchanged.

**Potential risks**  
Removing the standalone RMSNorm script drops the FlashInfer comparison baseline, so future RMSNorm performance regressions relative to FlashInfer can no longer be measured with the old tooling. Any external scripts, docs, or CI jobs that still reference the deleted files (cutlass_benchmarks, benchmark_rmsnorm.py, benchmark_hashing.py) will break. The shape-config comprehension change alters SHAPE_CONFIGS structure, so downstream consumers expecting only rms_norm keys must be verified.

**Key insights**  
The change is primarily a cleanup and consolidation effort rather than a functional feature, but it is classified SUBSTANTIAL because it deletes executable benchmark code and modifies a lint configuration. The test plan is reasonably thorough, including a smoke script that validates correctness and timing CSV generation for both RMSNorm ops on a B300. Reviewers should confirm no remaining references to the removed scripts exist in docs, CI, or other tooling, and that the IR harness genuinely covers the scenarios the old script handled.

  - `vllm/model_executor/models/qwen3_asr_realtime.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/qwen3_omni_moe_thinker.py`

  - `vllm/models/inkling/common/mm_preprocess.py`
  - `vllm/models/minimax_m3/common/mm_preprocess.py`
  - `vllm/multimodal/processing/context.py`
  - `vllm/multimodal/processing/processor.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This is a large refactor of the multimodal HF processor pipeline in vLLM. It renames MultiModalProcessingInfo to MultiModalProcessingResult (now carrying prompt_ids), replaces _get_hf_mm_data/_preprocess_hf_mm_data with a unified _get_hf_mm_inputs returning an HFMultiModalInputs NamedTuple (hf_data, hf_kwargs, passthrough_data), renames _get_hf_processor_text to _get_hf_mm_text, and splits _apply_hf_processor_main into four overridable hooks: _get_hf_mm_inputs, _call_hf_processor, _finalize_hf_mm_data, and _postprocess_hf_mm_data. It also centralizes the audios->audio remap, moves truncation=False to only apply when dummy text is present, and removes many model-specific _apply_hf_processor_main overrides across ~74 files.

**Technical impact**  
Consolidates multimodal input normalization and HF invocation into a consistent contract, reducing duplicated per-model boilerplate and making prompt postprocessing part of the cached/uncached result flow. Models that previously overrode _apply_hf_processor_main now override narrower hooks, and the cache-miss builder skips empty modalities. This changes behavior in several subtle ways: truncation is no longer globally defaulted, audios is centrally remapped to audio (with model-specific re-remaps where needed), and prompt_ids now flow through MultiModalProcessingResult.

**Potential risks**  
High blast radius across many models; subtle regressions are plausible where models relied on the old global truncation=False default, on the previous audios key naming, or on the exact ordering of passthrough merge vs postprocessing. Models with custom processors (Ultravox, Phi-4 MM, NanoNemotronVL, Dots3Note, Qwen Omni variants) had to re-add compatibility remaps, and any missed case could break audio or video handling. The _maybe_apply_prompt_updates signature change and MultiModalProcessingResult.prompt_ids addition touch core caching paths, so cache correctness and prompt-update application need careful verification. The PR description lacks a test plan and results, increasing uncertainty.

**Key insights**  
The refactor is well-structured around a clear four-stage contract and a single normalized input tuple, which should improve maintainability and reduce per-model duplication. The most important behavioral change to scrutinize is the removal of the global truncation=False default and its conditional reattachment only alongside dummy text, plus the central audios->audio remap. Reviewers should focus on models that previously overrode _apply_hf_processor_main and on the cache/prompt-update flow, and should request explicit test evidence given the breadth of the change.

## 46. [[ROCm] Expose kFp8DynamicTokenSym on AITER PTPC linears](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54248)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #54248
- **Author:** [rebklee](https://github.com/rebklee)
- **Merged By:** [AndreasKaratzas](https://github.com/AndreasKaratzas)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 09:48:30
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54248/files) (5):**
  - `tests/fusion/test_quant_activation_contract.py`
  - `tests/quantization/test_quark.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/scaled_mm/aiter.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/online/fp8.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/quark/schemes/quark_w8a8_fp8.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR exposes kFp8DynamicTokenSym via input_quant_key() on three AITER PTPC FP8 scaled-MM kernels (AiterPreshuffledPerTokenFp8ScaledMMLinearKernel, AiterPerTokenFp8ScaledMMLinearKernel, AiterHipbMMPerTokenFp8ScaledMMLinearKernel) so that expose_input_quant_key can mark layers and upstream producers can skip dynamic_per_token_scaled_quant. It also wires expose_input_quant_key into Fp8PtpcOnlineLinearMethod.process_weights_after_loading and QuarkW8A8Fp8.create_weights, and adjusts the batch-invariant dequant path to skip when x is already a QuantizedActivation.

**Technical impact**  
Enables the QuantizedActivation fusion contract for AITER PTPC linears on ROCm, allowing redundant per-token FP8 quantization to be elided when the activation is already quantized. The apply() change in online/fp8.py prevents double-dequantization when a QuantizedActivation is passed under VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT, and the QuarkW8A8Fp8.apply_weights signature is widened to accept QuantizedActivation. Tests extend the fusion contract suite and add a Quark-specific test verifying the layer advertises the kernel's input_quant_key.

**Potential risks**  
The new input_quant_key() implementations assume the kernels always consume per-token dynamic FP8 activations; if any AITER PTPC variant diverges (e.g., per-tensor or static activation paths), marking the layer could cause producers to skip quantization incorrectly and produce wrong numerics. The batch-invariant branch now bypasses dequantization when x is a QuantizedActivation, which changes behavior for that path and relies on the kernel handling the quantized input correctly. The new Quark test depends on AITER availability, tuned shapes, and monkeypatched TP rank, so it may be skipped or brittle across environments.

**Key insights**  
This is a targeted follow-up to prior QuantizedActivation contract work, closing the gap where AITER PTPC kernels returned None from input_quant_key and thus were never marked by expose_input_quant_key. The change is small but behavior-affecting because it enables a fusion optimization that skips redundant quantization, so correctness depends on the kernels genuinely consuming kFp8DynamicTokenSym activations. Test coverage is reasonable but environment-gated, so CI on non-ROCm or untuned shapes will not exercise the new path.

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Enables ruff's pydocstyle (D) ruleset in pyproject.toml and fixes the resulting violations across ~2000 files. D100-D107 are ignored so no new docstrings are required; six more rules (D205, D301, D400, D401, D404, D415) are ignored because their autofixes were wrong or churn-only. The bulk of the diff is mechanical autofix output (D209, D212, D200, D202, D413), plus ~290 hand-written Args: entries for D417, 807 trailing periods, and 37 E501 regressions fixed.

**Technical impact**  
Primarily a documentation/lint change with no intended runtime behavior change. However, the diff is enormous (+15050/-14295 across 2002 files) and touches production code in vllm/, including docstrings that are parsed at runtime by tools like argparse help generation, FastAPI endpoint documentation, and pydantic field descriptions. Some docstring rewrites also changed formatting of numpy-style Parameters blocks to Google-style Args, which could affect any tooling that parses those docstrings.

**Potential risks**  
The sheer size makes review impractical and raises the chance of accidental semantic edits hidden among mechanical changes. Docstrings used as CLI help text or API descriptions may render differently. The D212 autofix collapsing wrapped summaries onto line 1 introduced E501 regressions that were fixed by hand, indicating the autofix is not always safe. Any docstring that is actually consumed programmatically (e.g., by xgrammar, guidance, or plugin discovery) could be affected. There is also risk of merge conflicts for all in-flight branches.

**Key insights**  
This is a high-churn, low-logic PR that is best validated by confirming the ruff config is correct and that the diff contains no non-docstring code changes. The decision to ignore D205/D400/D415 is sound because their autofixes append periods to list items. The hand-written Args: entries for D417 are the only substantive content and should be spot-checked for accuracy, since incorrect parameter descriptions are worse than none. Consider splitting such sweeping lint enablement into smaller, reviewable chunks in the future.

## 48. [[Bugfix][HiSparse][PD] Align region-mapped pulls across logical block sizes](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57077)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57077
- **Author:** [NickLucche](https://github.com/NickLucche)
- **Merged By:** [NickLucche](https://github.com/NickLucche)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 09:39:07
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57077/files) (4):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_nixl_connector_hma.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/base_worker.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/metadata.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/nixl/pull_worker.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a NIXL region-mapped pull bug where producer allocation padding (extra physical pages from larger logical blocks) was mistaken for a cached prefix, shifting the selected transfer pages. The fix passes the producer's remote_num_tokens through RemoteMeta, computes per-region cached-prefix offsets, and selects physical pages by token position rather than list length. It also changes transfer completion to use the region read plan via a new region_blocks_to_zero field, zeroing only destination pages that were allocated but not covered by the read, instead of pairing groups positionally.

**Technical impact**  
Adds num_tokens to RemoteMeta and region_blocks_to_zero to ReqMeta, extends _apply_prefix_caching_by_region with optional num_computed_blocks/num_remote_blocks parameters, adds _zero_region_blocks in base_worker, and reworks pull_worker._read_blocks_for_req to compute matched local/remote regions and a zeroing plan. New guards raise NotImplementedError for mismatched physical block sizes or heterogeneous logical geometry lacking required metadata. Behavior for homogeneous/fallback paths is preserved, and 169 lines of CPU tests cover padding, unequal per-region cache hits, and strided-layer zeroing.

**Potential risks**  
The new token-position selection and zeroing logic is intricate and depends on correct remote_num_tokens and local_num_computed_blocks metadata; missing or stale values could silently mis-select pages. The NotImplementedError guards may break previously working heterogeneous configurations that relied on the old fallback. GPU accuracy validation is explicitly pending, and the PR depends on prerequisite #57049 for empty-receive fixes, so rebasing or partial application could introduce regressions. Zeroing via untyped_storage with computed offsets is sensitive to stride/base-address assumptions.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is that equal command-line block sizes do not imply equal logical/physical allocation between P and D, so region suffix matching must be driven by token positions rather than page counts. Separating completion cleanup from positional group pairing via a per-request region_blocks_to_zero plan correctly handles differing group layouts and allocation padding. The change is well-scoped with explicit rejection of unsupported geometries and a strong negative-control test, but it remains unvalidated on GPU and depends on an unmerged prerequisite.

## 49. [[P/D] Report prefill worker cache hits in prompt_tokens_details](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54222)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #54222
- **Author:** [robertgshaw2-redhat](https://github.com/robertgshaw2-redhat)
- **Merged By:** [markmc](https://github.com/markmc)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 08:23:32
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54222/files) (4):**
  - `tests/v1/engine/test_output_processor.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_nixl_connector.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/output_processor.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR fixes token accounting for prefill/decode (P/D) disaggregation when --enable-prompt-token-details is used. Previously the D worker reported ~100% cached tokens because KVs pulled from the remote P worker appeared as local cache hits; now the P worker exports its own prefix-cache hit count via kv_transfer_params['remote_prefill_cached_tokens'], and the D worker's OutputProcessor overrides num_cached_tokens with that value for accurate MaaS billing.

**Technical impact**  
Adds a new field remote_prefill_cached_tokens to RequestState, populated in from_new_request from sampling_params.extra_args.kv_transfer_params when do_remote_prefill is set. The scheduler now injects prefill_stats.num_cached_tokens into kv_transfer_params on request finish, and process_outputs overrides num_cached_tokens when the remote value is present. Two new tests cover the scheduler export path and the output processor override behavior for both remote and non-remote prefill cases.

**Potential risks**  
The override only triggers when do_remote_prefill is truthy and remote_prefill_cached_tokens is an int, so non-P/D paths are unaffected. However, correctness depends on the P worker's prefill_stats being populated and the kv_transfer_params being propagated end-to-end; if any connector or serialization path drops the new key, the D worker silently falls back to the old (incorrect) local count. There is also a subtle coupling between scheduler and output_processor assumptions about when prefill_stats is available on the finishing output.

**Key insights**  
The fix correctly separates the P worker's genuine cache hit rate from the D worker's artificially high local hit rate caused by KV transfer, which matters for accurate token accounting in MaaS deployments. The change is narrowly scoped with explicit guards and is well covered by targeted unit tests, but end-to-end validation across real P/D connectors (e.g., NIXL) would strengthen confidence that the new kv_transfer_params key survives serialization.

## 50. [[Model] Qwen4Exp: fp8_e4m3 main KV cache on the QSA path](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55557)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #55557
- **Author:** [semerandre](https://github.com/semerandre)
- **Merged By:** [ZJY0516](https://github.com/ZJY0516)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 08:07:52
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55557/files) (3):**
  - `tests/models/qwen4_exp/test_qsa_reference.py`
  - `vllm/models/qwen4_exp/nvidia/ops/qsa.py`
  - `vllm/models/qwen4_exp/nvidia/qsa.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR enables fp8_e4m3 storage for the main K/V cache of Qwen3.8-Flash-Next QSA (sparse attention) layers, which was previously rejected. It adds an IS_FP8 path to the split-K sparse paged attention Triton kernel that loads fp8 pages and applies per-tensor K/V dequant scales, updates the QSA backend to advertise and accept fp8/fp8_e4m3 cache dtypes, and adds an fp8 correctness test against the bf16 reference. The goal is to roughly halve per-token KV footprint (reported pool growth from ~435K to ~772K tokens).

**Technical impact**  
The kernel now takes softmax_scale and output_scale as runtime args, folding the K dequant scale into the attention scale and the V dequant scale into a per-row reciprocal normalizer, so no device scale buffers are needed. The backend bypasses the parent flash-attn fp8 probe by passing 'auto' to the parent constructor and restoring the real dtype afterward, and reinterprets the uint8 cache as e4m3 at attention time. An sm_120-specific tuning table is added, split by cache dtype, with the GB300 table retained as default. Tests add fp8 params (scales 0.5/2.0) alongside existing bf16 cases.

**Potential risks**  
The parent-constructor dtype swap is fragile: it relies on the parent only using kv_cache_dtype for the probe and on do_kv_cache_update reading the attribute at call time, so future parent changes could break it. The uint8-to-e4m3 view assumes identical itemsize and preserved shape/strides. The sm_120 tuning table is hardware-specific and may regress other architectures if _is_sm120 detection is wrong. Reported accuracy is validated only via prompt-logprob traces and needle tests on one GPU, with no md5 comparison possible due to existing bf16 run-to-run nondeterminism, so subtle fp8 drift could be masked.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped to fp8 only, leaving nvfp4 and indexer side caches (bf16) for follow-up PRs, and explicitly supersedes the stale #54846. The host-side scale folding is a clean design that avoids device scale buffers, and the test deliberately uses non-unit, asymmetric scales to catch k/v swaps. The main review attention should go to the parent-constructor workaround and the sm_120 tuning table, since both are brittle integration points rather than the core kernel math.

## 51. [[BugFix][PCP] Handle missing DP metadata in one-sided EP](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54016)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #54016
- **Author:** [LopezCastroRoberto](https://github.com/LopezCastroRoberto)
- **Merged By:** [LucasWilkinson](https://github.com/LucasWilkinson)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 07:25:00
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54016/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/fused_moe/prepare_finalize/flashinfer_nvlink_one_sided.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The get_local_sizes() helper in the FlashInfer NVLink one-sided EP path previously asserted that ForwardContext.dp_metadata was not None. With DP=1 (e.g., TP=1, PCP=4, EP), dp_metadata can legitimately be None, causing an assertion failure during init or execution. The fix returns None in that case and relies on the caller's existing fallback to the local token count (a1.shape[0]), while preserving behavior for DP>1.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the function signature to return list[int] \\| None and replaces a hard assertion with a graceful None return. This enables the one-sided EP path to operate correctly when data parallelism is disabled, deferring size determination to the caller's fallback logic. Behavior for DP>1 is unchanged since dp_metadata is still used when present.

**Potential risks**  
The fix depends on the caller correctly handling a None return and falling back to a1.shape[0]; if any other caller of get_local_sizes() assumes a non-None list, it could raise a TypeError. The PR description mentions added tests for both missing and available DP metadata, but the provided diff only shows the single source file change, so test coverage cannot be verified from this excerpt.

**Key insights**  
This is a targeted bug fix for a real configuration (TP=1, PCP=4, EP with DP=1) where the prior assertion was too strict. The minimal +3/-2 change is low-risk given the caller already has a fallback path, but reviewers should confirm all call sites handle None and that the claimed tests are actually included in the PR.

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR enables LoRA support for ModernBertModel pooling models by making the class implement the SupportsLoRA interface and adding a packed_modules_mapping for Wqkv and Wi modules. It also updates the pooling models documentation to mark ModernBertModel as LoRA-compatible. This addresses issue #57002.

**Technical impact**  
ModernBertModel now advertises LoRA capability to vLLM's LoRA machinery, allowing LoRA adapters to be applied to its attention and MLP projections. The packed_modules_mapping tells the LoRA loader how to map fused/packed weight names, which is required for correct adapter application.

**Potential risks**  
The packed_modules_mapping entries map each module name to itself, which may or may not match the actual fused weight layout in ModernBertModel; if incorrect, LoRA adapters could fail to load or apply to the wrong weights. The PR description lacks test plan and results, so runtime correctness is unverified. Only the LoRA column is marked supported in docs while the other column remains blank, which may be intentional but is worth confirming.

**Key insights**  
This is a small but behavior-changing change (7 lines in code) that unlocks a new capability rather than a trivial docs edit. The correctness hinges on the packed_modules_mapping being accurate for ModernBert's fused QKV and MLP weights. Reviewers should verify the mapping against the model's actual parameter names and request test evidence since the PR template sections are empty.

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR adds support for PEFT sequence-classification LoRA adapters that store a full classification head (score or classifier) via modules_to_save, which vLLM previously rejected. It introduces LoRAFullModuleWeights, a ClassificationHeadWithLoRA wrapper, loader-side separation of full head tensors from LoRA A/B tensors, validation of head shapes, and request-level routing of full linear weights through new punica apply_lora_full_linear methods. It also updates docs, examples, tests, and CI config.

**Technical impact**  
The change touches core LoRA loading, model manager activation, punica wrappers for GPU/CPU/XPU, PEFT helper validation, and the pooling classifier path. It enables mixed adapters with both backbone LoRA and full classification heads, adds shape validation against the runtime base head, and routes full heads via prompt-level mappings rather than token-level LoRA mappings. The new abstract method apply_lora_full_linear must be implemented by all punica wrappers, and the classifier replacement mutates the pooler's head in place.

**Potential risks**  
The PR is large and modifies inference-critical paths, so regressions in non-classification LoRA or pooling models are possible. The in-place replace_classifier mutation and the assumption of exactly one ClassificationHeadWithLoRA could break models with unusual pooler structures. The new punica method is abstract, so any out-of-tree or future wrapper must implement it. The draft description notes remaining work, and the truncated diff prevents full verification of activation, slot reuse, LRU eviction, and reset handling. Test coverage relies on specific HF adapters and fixed expected logits, which may be brittle across hardware or dependency updates.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly separates full module weights from LoRA A/B weights and uses request-level routing for classification heads, which is appropriate since classification outputs are per-request rather than per-token. Validation of weight and bias shapes against the runtime head is a good safety measure. The change to parse_fine_tuned_lora_name to recognize score/classifier weight and bias tensors is a key enabler. The PR is substantial and should be reviewed carefully for edge cases in adapter activation, eviction, and mixed-adapter scenarios before merging.

## 54. [[CI] Bump Transformers version to 5.17.0](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56108)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56108
- **Author:** [hmellor](https://github.com/hmellor)
- **Merged By:** [vllm-bot](https://github.com/vllm-bot)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 06:34:17
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56108/files) (17):**
  - `requirements/test/cpu.txt`
  - `requirements/test/cuda.in`
  - `requirements/test/cuda.txt`
  - `requirements/test/nightly-torch.txt`
  - `requirements/test/rocm.in`
  - `requirements/test/rocm.txt`
  - `requirements/test/xpu.in`
  - `requirements/test/xpu.txt`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/laguna.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/pixtral.py`
  - `vllm/models/qwen4_exp/amd/model.py`
  - `vllm/models/qwen4_exp/config.py`
  - `vllm/models/qwen4_exp/nvidia/model.py`
  - `vllm/transformers_utils/config.py`
  - `vllm/transformers_utils/configs/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/transformers_utils/configs/laguna.py`
  - `vllm/transformers_utils/configs/mellum.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR bumps the Transformers test dependency from 5.16.1 to 5.17.0 across all test requirement files and adapts vLLM model code to upstream API changes. It removes vLLM's local LagunaConfig and MellumConfig in favor of the upstream Transformers configs, adds a nested rope_parameters validation patch for Laguna, and updates Laguna, Pixtral, and Qwen4Exp model implementations to match the new Transformers behavior.

**Technical impact**  
Laguna now relies on the upstream Transformers LagunaConfig and uses mlp_layer_types instead of mlp_only_layers/decoder_sparse_step, with a hard error if the config predates 5.17.0. Pixtral conditionally imports either PixtralVisionRotaryEmbedding (axial RoPE) or the legacy PixtralRotaryEmbedding based on the installed Transformers version, and Qwen4Exp adds support for the new qwen_sparse_attention layer type alongside legacy full_attention QSA detection. A new monkeypatch drops shared top-level entries from nested rope_parameters dicts during config validation.

**Potential risks**  
The version-gated Pixtral import and meshgrid position-id logic could break if the upstream 5.17 API differs from the assumed names or shapes, and the new nested rope validation patch mutates config dicts in place, potentially masking malformed configs. Removing local LagunaConfig/MellumConfig means older Transformers versions or checkpoints relying on vLLM's definitions will fail, and the Laguna MoE layer selection now depends on the upstream mlp_layer_types ordering being correct. The renormalize=True and attention_bias changes in Laguna also alter behavior for existing checkpoints.

**Key insights**  
This is a coordinated dependency bump that shifts several model configs and attention-type handling to upstream Transformers, so correctness depends on the exact 5.17.0 API surface. The Pixtral compatibility shim and Qwen4Exp dual layer-type support are the most fragile parts and deserve targeted tests against both old and new Transformers versions. The Laguna changes intentionally drop support for pre-5.17 configs, which is a deliberate breaking change that should be documented for users.

## 55. [[Bugfix][Kimi-K3] Do not classify a stateless first chunk as a decode](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51483)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #51483
- **Author:** [sashko-zakharchuk](https://github.com/sashko-zakharchuk)
- **Merged By:** [ZJY0516](https://github.com/ZJY0516)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 06:24:49
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51483/files) (2):**
  - `tests/models/kimi_k3/test_kda_metadata.py`
  - `vllm/models/kimi_k3/nvidia/kda_metadata.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The Kimi-K3 KDA metadata builder previously classified any one-token row as a decode, so a stateless first chunk could take the pure-decode route and read recycled conv/recurrent state without initialization. The fix classifies by state (seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound <= query_lens_cpu, masked by is_prefilling) instead of query length, re-keys the cudagraph staging guard to the dispatcher's shape-based condition, and excludes cudagraph padding from num_prefills/num_prefill_tokens. Tests were added to pin each guard.

**Technical impact**  
Correctness fix for a silent state-inheritance bug: a freshly admitted one-token request no longer reads stale KDA state from a recycled block. It also changes routing for mixed batches, since once num_prefills > 0 the whole non-spec group takes the prefill route for that step, moving otherwise all-decode batches off ops.fused_kda_decode in eager, PIECEWISE, and uncaptured sizes. The cudagraph staging guard change is a strict no-op on base and preserves decode-graph replay behavior.

**Potential risks**  
The fix does not close the residual gap where an all-one-token batch is still dispatched to the captured decode graph on shape alone under FULL_AND_PIECEWISE, so replayed graphs remain unchanged. It is also a no-op on microbatching and context-parallel paths lacking is_prefilling, and it may interact with the draft PR #50855 that computes all_initial_states_fresh from the same distinction. The blast-radius note about moving mixed batches onto the prefill route could affect performance or expose latent prefill-path issues.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is a classification mismatch between query length and actual state ownership, compounded by Mamba blocks not being zeroed on reallocation. The fix correctly distinguishes stateless first chunks from resumed one-token chunks, which treat_short_extends_as_decodes=False alone would mishandle. The cudagraph staging guard must match the dispatcher's shape-based decision, not the metadata's num_prefills, to avoid replaying with stale staged indices.

## 56. [[Perf][GDN] Scatter mixed speculative outputs into the caller buffer](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57140)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57140
- **Author:** [tripathiarpan20](https://github.com/tripathiarpan20)
- **Merged By:** [ZJY0516](https://github.com/ZJY0516)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 06:03:58
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57140/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/model_executor/layers/mamba/gdn/qwen_gdn_linear_attn.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
In the Qwen GDN _forward_core path, the mixed speculative/non-speculative merge previously allocated a temporary merged_out tensor, scattered both output partitions into it, and copied the whole tensor into core_attn_out. The PR replaces this with two index_copy_ calls that write the speculative and non-speculative outputs directly into the caller's core_attn_out buffer, eliminating one output-sized allocation and the final full-output copy.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior is intended to be equivalent: token order, buffer aliases, and padding rows are preserved, and recurrent computation/state updates are untouched. The change removes an allocation of num_actual_tokens x value_heads x value_head_dim elements and one full-output copy per mixed batch, which the PR's isolated component timings show roughly halves merge time (e.g., 35.05 to 17.95 us at 32 tokens, 109.37 to 75.60 us at 4096 tokens eager). Serving measurements show workload-dependent throughput changes (+3.92% at batch 4, +4.10% at batch 8, -1.01% at batch 16, -0.46% at batch 32) and mixed TTFT results, so the model-level benefit is not uniform.

**Potential risks**  
The new code writes directly into core_attn_out, so correctness depends on spec_token_indx and non_spec_token_indx being disjoint and within bounds, and on core_attn_out having the expected shape/stride and being writable in place; any aliasing or overlap with the source tensors would silently corrupt output. The squeeze(0) calls assume the spec/non-spec tensors have a leading batch dimension of 1, and the change relies on index_copy_ semantics matching the previous index_copy_ along dim 1. The PR adds no new tests, and the serving benchmark shows higher tail latency at batches 16 and 32, so regressions at larger batch sizes or on other GPUs/models cannot be ruled out from the provided evidence.

**Key insights**  
This is a small, focused optimization (3 added, 7 removed lines) with a clear rationale and unusually thorough validation for its size, including real-kernel component comparisons, CUDA graph replay checks, and a serving benchmark. The strongest evidence is the exact baseline/patched equality in the component harnesses and the isolated merge timings; the serving results are noisy and workload-dependent, and the PR itself appropriately frames them as observed differences rather than a guaranteed speedup. The main review focus should be on index disjointness/bounds and in-place write safety, since the change trades a safe temporary buffer for direct mutation of the caller's output.

## 57. [[Bugfix][Mooncake] Report request-level KV load failures under HMA](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56855)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56855
- **Author:** [NickLucche](https://github.com/NickLucche)
- **Merged By:** [NickLucche](https://github.com/NickLucche)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 05:55:40
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56855/files) (9):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_kv_load_failure_recovery.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_mooncake_connector_hma.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_mooncake_store_connector.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/test_mooncake_store_worker.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/utils.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/mooncake/mooncake_connector.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/mooncake/store/connector.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/mooncake/store/worker.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR reverts #50388 and changes how KV load failures are reported under hybrid (HMA) KV cache layouts. Instead of teaching the scheduler to unpack multi-group block ID tuples (which is inherently ambiguous since block IDs are only unique within a group), the MooncakeConnector and MooncakeStoreConnector now report request-level failures via KVConnectorTransferResults.failed_recving when HMA is required, mirroring the Nixl approach from #35264. Non-HMA layouts keep the existing block-level failure reporting. The scheduler now raises a RuntimeError if block-level invalid_block_ids are reported on a multi-group layout, enforcing the new contract.

**Technical impact**  
Adds get_transfer_results() to both Mooncake connectors and their workers, introduces an _is_hma_required flag derived from scheduler config and transfer groups, and routes failure reporting accordingly. The scheduler's _update_requests_with_invalid_blocks loses its multi-group handling branch and _handle_invalid_blocks now fails closed on multi-group layouts. This removes the previously added scheduler-side unpacking robustness and shifts responsibility to connectors, which is a behavioral contract change for any connector reporting block-level failures under HMA.

**Potential risks**  
The new RuntimeError in _handle_invalid_blocks is a hard failure mode: any connector (including third-party ones) that still reports block-level invalid_block_ids on a multi-group layout will now crash the scheduler instead of being handled. The _is_hma_required derivation must stay consistent between worker and scheduler, and any divergence could cause failures to be reported through the wrong path. Reverting #50388 removes the crash fix for the unpacking path, so correctness now depends entirely on connectors correctly detecting HMA and using failed_recving.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is that block-level failure reporting is fundamentally ambiguous under HMA because block IDs are only unique within a group, so the fix is to report at request granularity rather than patch the unpacking. The PR deliberately trades scheduler-side robustness for a cleaner contract, with tests covering both HMA and non-HMA paths for both connectors. The fail-closed RuntimeError is a strong signal that this is an intentional interface requirement, not just a bug fix.

## 58. [[Bugfix] Let an optional Literal flag accept the None it advertises](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52370)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #52370
- **Author:** [vineethsaivs](https://github.com/vineethsaivs)
- **Merged By:** [hmellor](https://github.com/hmellor)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 05:14:55
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52370/files) (2):**
  - `tests/engine/test_arg_utils.py`
  - `vllm/engine/arg_utils.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR fixes a CLI bug in vllm/engine/arg_utils.py where optional Literal flags advertised 'None' as a valid choice but rejected it. argparse applies the type converter before checking choices, so optional_type converted 'None' and '' to the object None while the registered choice remained the string 'None'. The fix appends the object None to choices instead of the string, and updates a metavar join to stringify choices so help output stays identical.

**Technical impact**  
Behavioral change limited to argument parsing: 16 flags that previously errored on 'None' or '' now accept those values as documented. Help text is byte-identical because argparse str()s choices when rendering. The test suite is refactored to share a get_kwargs fixture and adds a parametrized test pinning both None spellings, a real choice, and continued help advertisement.

**Potential risks**  
The change relies on argparse's internal behavior of stringifying choices for help and comparing converted values against choices; this is stable but implicit. Any downstream code that inspects kwargs['choices'] expecting only strings (e.g., other metavar construction or config serialization) could break, though the PR updates the one known join site. The new test could not be run via normal pytest collection due to an unrelated xgrammar segfault, so CI coverage depends on that environment issue being resolved.

**Key insights**  
The bug was a type mismatch between the converted value (None object) and the registered choice (string 'None'), making the error message self-contradictory. The minimal fix aligns the choice with the converter's output rather than changing the converter, preserving help output. The PR is well-scoped, includes a regression test, and explicitly distinguishes itself from nearby argparse_utils work.

## 59. [[Model] Optimize Sarvam MLA routing and preserve FP32 router logits](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56034)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56034
- **Author:** [harshit-sarvam](https://github.com/harshit-sarvam)
- **Merged By:** [zyongye](https://github.com/zyongye)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 05:02:48
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56034/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/sarvam.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
In vllm/model_executor/models/sarvam.py, the Sarvam MLA router was changed from a plain nn.Linear with manual input/output dtype casts to the existing GateLinear layer with out_dtype=router_dtype, and a new _remap_config static method now defaults missing or None n_group and topk_group config values to 1. The stated purpose is to enable the supported TRTLLM MoE path for the flat top-8-of-128 routing and to stop narrowing FP32 router logits to BF16 before expert selection, improving router overhead and routing precision.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior changes in two ways: MoE backend selection can shift from CUTLASS to TRTLLM when the flat-routing defaults apply, and expert selection now operates on FP32 logits rather than BF16-narrowed logits, which the PR reports changes selected expert sets in roughly 2.2% of tested layer/input comparisons. The PR reports measured decode speedups of about 1.03-1.08x from GateLinear and 1.11-1.17x from the backend switch, plus prefill gains, but these GPU numbers predate the latest upstream merge and are not a fresh evaluation of the merged head.

**Potential risks**  
The change is intentionally not bit-exact with the previous path, since retaining FP32 logits alters close top-k routing decisions; the PR acknowledges this and notes the disagreement counts measure expert-set differences, not task error rates. The config remapping mutates the HF config in place and could mask genuinely different checkpoint routing settings or interact with other code reading n_group/topk_group. Performance and repeatability evidence is historical and from different nodes, so post-merge validation on the actual head is still required, and determinism at higher concurrency is not established.

**Key insights**  
The dominant numerical change comes from removing the logit downcast rather than from switching the gate to BF16, per the router experiment table. The flat-routing defaults are the boundary that flips the MoE backend and produced the largest measured throughput gain, while the GateLinear change adds a smaller incremental speedup. The PR is transparent about limited fresh validation and relies on separate quality evals for parity, so reviewers should focus on config-mutation semantics and confirm the TRTLLM path is correct for all Sarvam checkpoints, not just the tested one.

No runtime, build, or test behavior changes; these are markdown docs consumed only by agent tooling. They document existing interfaces and conventions, so they may influence future agent-generated contributions but do not alter the codebase itself.

**Potential risks**  
Docs can drift out of sync with code over time, and inaccurate guidance could mislead agents into wrong edits (e.g., the noted inconsistency of the '_engine_' suffix). Since content is descriptive and references were checked against main, immediate risk is low.

**Key insights**  
The PR codifies important conventions: new parsers should be unified Parser implementations in vllm/parser/ with legacy reasoning/tool parsers kept as thin adapters, and the legacy interfaces must not change due to out-of-tree plugins. It also flags performance-sensitive paths like is_reasoning_end_streaming running per decode step. No functional code is touched, so this is a documentation-only change.
