## 61. [[Platform] Move env check function to platform interface](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48599)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #48599
- **Author:** [wangxiyuan](https://github.com/wangxiyuan)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 03:08:36
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48599/files) (3):**
  - `vllm/engine/arg_utils.py`
  - `vllm/envs.py`
  - `vllm/platforms/interface.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The environment variable validation function validate_environ was moved from vllm/envs.py into the Platform interface (vllm/platforms/interface.py) as a classmethod, and arg_utils.py now calls current_platform.validate_environ instead of envs.validate_environ. This allows platform plugins (e.g., vllm-ascend) to override the check so their own VLLM_ASCEND_* env vars no longer trigger misleading 'Unknown vLLM environment variable' warnings.

**Technical impact**  
Behavior is preserved for the default Platform implementation, which replicates the original logic using envs.environment_variables and os.environ. The change introduces a new extension point on the Platform interface, enabling subclasses to customize env validation. It also adds a local import of vllm.envs inside the method to avoid circular imports, and relies on os and logger already being available in interface.py.

**Potential risks**  
Any external code or tests that imported or called vllm.envs.validate_environ directly will break since the function was removed without a deprecation shim. The local import of vllm.envs inside the classmethod could mask circular-import issues or add minor overhead per call. Platform subclasses that override validate_environ must ensure they still catch genuinely unknown VLLM_* variables to avoid silently suppressing real misconfigurations.

**Key insights**  
This is a clean refactor that converts a module-level utility into an overridable platform hook, improving extensibility for out-of-tree platform plugins. The diff is small (+19/-10 across 3 files) but changes a public-ish API surface (envs.validate_environ removal), so downstream consumers should be checked. No tests are included in the PR, which is a gap given the behavior-sensitive nature of env validation.

## 62. [[Model][DSv4.1] FlashMLA mega attention and the NVFP4 compressed KV cache](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56935)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56935
- **Author:** [zyongye](https://github.com/zyongye)
- **Merged By:** [ywang96](https://github.com/ywang96)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 03:07:51
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56935/files) (20):**
  - `benchmarks/kernels/benchmark_dsv41_mega_attn.py`
  - `csrc/libtorch_stable/fused_deepseek_v4_qnorm_rope_kv_insert_kernel.cu`
  - `csrc/libtorch_stable/ops.h`
  - `csrc/libtorch_stable/torch_bindings.cpp`
  - `tests/kernels/test_compressor_kv_cache.py`
  - `tests/kernels/test_dsv41_mega_attn_layouts.py`
  - `tests/kernels/test_fused_deepseek_v4_qnorm_rope_kv_insert.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/amd/rocm.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/attention.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/common/ops/cache_utils.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/common/ops/fused_compress_quant_cache.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/common/ops/fused_layout.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/dspark.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/flash_mla_mega_attn.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/flashinfer_sparse.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/flashmla.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/nvidia/model.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v41/sparse_mla.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/mla/sparse_swa.py`
  - `vllm/v1/attention/backends/registry.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new FlashMLA mega-attention backend (FLASHMLA_MEGA_ATTN_DSV41) for DeepSeek V4.1 on SM100 that fuses Q RoPE, sparse attention, output inverse RoPE and FP8 cast into one kernel, plus a new 288-byte NVFP4 compressed KV cache record. It extends the fused qnorm/rope/kv-insert CUDA op with apply_q_rope and is_q_interleaved knobs, introduces weight permutations for wq_b/wo_a, and makes mega attention the default SM100 backend when the topology allows it.

**Technical impact**  
Substantial behavior change: the default attention backend on SM100 shifts to the mega kernel, the KV cache dtype resolution now supports nvfp4_ds_mla and packed-format aliasing, and the attention layer interface gains accepts_unnormed_unroped_query and packed_kv_cache_dtype contracts. The fused CUDA op signature changes (two new params, defaulted for backward compatibility), a new Triton NVFP4 insert/gather path is added, and DSpark draft layers now run attention weight finalization. Reported decode speedups up to 1.45x at TP1 with a 45%-smaller compressed record and no measurable accuracy loss.

**Potential risks**  
Prefill performance is explicitly unmeasured, so prefill regressions are unknown. The default-backend switch on SM100 affects all users unless they opt out, and the is_available_for gating depends on runtime kernel availability and topology checks that could mis-select on unusual configs. Two commits in the series do not build standalone, complicating bisection. The new NVFP4 record and Triton gather path lack a CuteDSL equivalent and are only exercised by new tests; the wo_a permutation changes summation order (acknowledged ulp-level differences). Non-SM100 arches are untouched but the default declines there, which could surprise users expecting the new path.

**Key insights**  
The design cleanly separates orthogonal Q knobs (apply_q_rope runtime, is_q_interleaved template) and validates their independence with a bit-exact orthogonality test. Weight permutations are applied once at load rather than per step, and a single output buffer pair per step lets one fp8_einsum cover prefill and decode. The PR is unusually well-documented with benchmark tables, accuracy comparisons against a history band, and explicit statements of what is not covered (prefill perf, non-SM100). The rebase interaction with #56441 is handled at call sites that git did not flag as conflicts, which is a notable maintenance hazard worth watching.

- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 02:17:08

- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55988/files) (4):**

  - `vllm/model_executor/models/deepencoder.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/mimo_v2_omni.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/step3p5.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The PR removes five dead no-op self-assignments across vLLM model files, as described in the title and description. Most changes are pure dead-code removal, but the routed_experts.py change also restructures the conditional logic by merging the quant_method_name check with the is_transposed check into a single condition, which is a behavior-preserving refactor rather than a simple no-op removal. The description claims zero behavior change, but the routed_experts.py diff shows a non-trivial control-flow rewrite.

**Technical impact**  
The changes affect weight loading in fused MoE layers, patch embedding fallback in deepencoder, and variable handling in mimo_v2_omni and step3p5. The routed_experts.py refactor changes the structure of the conditional but should preserve semantics: previously the transposed weight was applied only when both conditions held, and now the same combined condition gates the same operation. The deepencoder.py inversion preserves the fallback to self.patch_embedding when patch_embeds is None. The other removals are genuinely dead code.

**Potential risks**  
The routed_experts.py change is the highest risk because it rewrites a nested conditional into a combined condition; any subtle difference in operator precedence or short-circuit behavior could alter weight loading for compressed-tensors checkpoints. The description's claim of zero behavior change is slightly overstated for this file, since the diff is a refactor, not just a deletion. The other changes are low risk but rely on the assumption that the removed assignments were truly no-ops, which appears correct from the diffs.

**Key insights**  
Most of the PR is trivial dead-code cleanup, but the routed_experts.py hunk is a substantive refactor that should be reviewed carefully for semantic equivalence. The description's table omits the routed_experts.py restructuring, focusing only on the no-op removals, which may understate the scope of the change. CI and existing tests should cover the affected paths, but the weight_loader logic in fused MoE is performance- and correctness-sensitive, so a targeted test for transposed compressed-tensors checkpoints would be prudent.

## 65. [[Bugfix][CPU] Fall back to CpuPlatform when zentorch fails to import](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54923)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #54923
- **Author:** [ganeshr10](https://github.com/ganeshr10)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 02:15:12
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54923/files) (2):**
  - `tests/test_zen_cpu_platform_detection.py`
  - `vllm/platforms/__init__.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
The cpu_platform_plugin() in vllm/platforms/__init__.py previously only caught ImportError around 'import zentorch', so an ABI-mismatched zentorch build raising OSError (undefined symbol) escaped the handler, leaving the platform unresolved and causing a confusing 'Failed to infer device type' startup failure. This PR adds an explicit 'except OSError' branch that logs a warning and falls back to CpuPlatform, deliberately keeping the catch narrow per reviewer feedback rather than catching blanket Exception. Tests were updated and expanded to cover the new fallback, the non-OSError propagation path, and the plugin-failure logging path.

**Technical impact**  
On AMD Zen CPUs with a broken/ABI-mismatched zentorch, platform detection now resolves to vllm.platforms.cpu.CpuPlatform instead of UnspecifiedPlatform, allowing the server to start rather than aborting. The change is behavior-affecting for the failure path only; successful zentorch imports and non-OSError failures (e.g. RuntimeError) still propagate as before, preserving fail-fast semantics for unknown errors. The added tests pin both the recovery and the narrowness of the catch.

**Potential risks**  
Catching OSError is broader than just the documented undefined-symbol ABI mismatch; other OSErrors raised during zentorch import (e.g. missing dependent shared libraries, permission issues, or genuinely broken installs) will now be silently downgraded to a CPU fallback with only a warning, potentially masking real configuration problems. Users may unknowingly run on CpuPlatform with degraded performance instead of being alerted to fix their zentorch install. The fallback relies on exc_info logging being visible, which is only guaranteed at WARNING level and above.

**Key insights**  
This is the behavioral half of a two-part fix, complementing the earlier logging-only change (#52285). The design choice to catch only OSError rather than Exception is a reasonable balance between recoverability and fail-fast, but it is worth confirming that all OSError variants during zentorch import are truly safe to recover from. The test suite appropriately distinguishes the recoverable OSError case from the non-recoverable RuntimeError case, which is good practice for pinning exception-handling scope.

## 66. [[Perf][DSV4.1] Pad shared experts for native MegaMoE fusion](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56568)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56568
- **Author:** [gcanlin](https://github.com/gcanlin)
- **Merged By:** [Isotr0py](https://github.com/Isotr0py)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 02:08:43
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56568/files) (2):**
  - `tests/models/test_deepseek_v4_mega_moe.py`
  - `vllm/models/deepseek_v4/nvidia/model.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR pads DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash's 2304-wide shared expert weights and scales with zeros and unit scales so they align with MegaMoE's 2560-wide routed expert layout, enabling native shared-expert fusion across all 40 layers. It modifies vllm/models/deepseek_v4/nvidia/model.py to add padding logic and preserve checkpoint-shaped parameters, and updates tests/models/test_deepseek_v4_mega_moe.py to cover padded, fallback, and mxfp8 cases.

**Technical impact**  
Enables fusion of shared experts with routed experts in the MegaMoE backend, yielding measured throughput gains of roughly 10-18% and TPOT reductions of 8-14% on 8xB200. The implementation keeps original checkpoint-shaped weights allocated for generic post-load hooks, increasing model-load memory by about 1.87 GiB per rank, and retains the unsupported-layout fallback path.

**Potential risks**  
The added memory overhead (~1.87 GiB/rank) could matter on tighter GPU memory configurations. Padding correctness depends on dtype, shape, and scale-dtype guards; edge cases with non-standard block sizes or scale layouts could silently fall back or mis-pad. The test changes rely on monkeypatched fakes, so real DeepGEMM behavior differences may not be fully captured.

**Key insights**  
The padding is applied separately to gate/up halves to preserve the SwiGLU midpoint split, and unit scales (value 1.0) are used for padded scale regions. When padding is active, transformed weights are stored separately rather than overwriting the linear weight data, preserving checkpoint-shaped parameters for generic hooks. The PR includes thorough benchmarking and GSM8K validation showing negligible accuracy impact.

## 68. [[XPU][Bugfix] Fix Qwen2-Audio ValueError on audio clips longer than 30s](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56912)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56912
- **Author:** [jbyczkow](https://github.com/jbyczkow)
- **Merged By:** [jikunshang](https://github.com/jikunshang)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 01:54:02
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56912/files) (1):**
  - `vllm/model_executor/models/qwen2_audio.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
A single line adds truncation=True to the multimodal kwargs returned by Qwen2AudioMultiModalProcessor._preprocess_hf_mm_data(). This overrides the truncation=False default that call_hf_processor() applies via setdefault, which was leaking into the Whisper-style feature extractor and preventing audio clips longer than 30s from being truncated to the model's fixed 3000-frame window.

**Technical impact**  
Restores the feature extractor's intended truncation/padding behavior so input_features always match the fixed 3000 mel-frame shape expected by Qwen2AudioFeatureInputs. This prevents the ValueError on long audio clips that previously crashed the engine core and caused EngineDeadError, and it does not affect the dummy placeholder text since that sequence is tiny and never truncated either way.

**Potential risks**  
The fix is narrowly scoped to Qwen2-Audio preprocessing, but it hardcodes truncation=True for all audio inputs, so any future use case relying on untruncated long-form audio features would be silently clipped. There is no automated regression test added in this PR, so the fix relies on manual verification and could regress if upstream kwarg handling changes again.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was a kwarg collision between text-side truncation semantics and audio-side feature extraction defaults, introduced by an earlier refactor (0b19ebc). The minimal one-line override is an appropriate targeted fix, but adding a unit or integration test with a >30s clip would guard against future regressions in the shared call_hf_processor path.

## 69. [[Feature][Frontend] Expose effective attention block size for DCP](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56538)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56538
- **Author:** [JulienDarve](https://github.com/JulienDarve)
- **Merged By:** [BugenZhao](https://github.com/BugenZhao)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 01:29:44
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56538/files) (18):**
  - `docs/serving/context_parallel_deployment.md`
  - `rust/Cargo.toml`
  - `rust/proto/README.md`
  - `rust/proto/control.proto`
  - `rust/src/engine-core-client/src/client.rs`
  - `rust/src/engine-core-client/src/mock_engine.rs`
  - `rust/src/engine-core-client/src/protocol/handshake.rs`
  - `rust/src/engine-core-client/src/tests/client.rs`
  - `rust/src/engine-core-client/src/tests/python_compat.py`
  - `rust/src/server/src/grpc/control.rs`
  - `rust/src/server/src/grpc/tests.rs`
  - `tests/config/test_config_utils.py`
  - `tests/v1/core/test_kv_cache_utils.py`
  - `tests/v1/engine/test_engine_core_client.py`
  - `vllm/config/cache.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/core.py`
  - `vllm/v1/engine/core_client.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new optional CacheConfig.effective_attention_block_size field that reports the DCP-adjusted full-attention KV cache block size (physical block size times DCP world size). The value is computed in EngineCore from the initialized KV cache managers, propagated through EngineCoreReadyResponse, aggregated across engines in the Rust client, and exposed via gRPC Control.GetServerInfo. Documentation and proto schema were updated accordingly.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a new cross-language protocol field (proto field 14, optional uint64) and a new Python config attribute, touching Python engine core, engine client, Rust engine-core-client, and gRPC server. The field is excluded from CacheConfig.compute_hash to avoid invalidating compilation caches, and aggregation logic returns None when engines disagree or the value is unavailable, preserving backward compatibility with older engines.

**Potential risks**  
The Rust client's effective_attention_block_size() requires all engines to report the identical value, so heterogeneous or partially-upgraded deployments silently return None. The Python MPClient aggregation uses a set of observed sizes and never resets, so a transient disagreement permanently pins the value to None for that client instance. The proto field number 14 is added out of sequence (after 11), which is valid but worth confirming against reserved ranges. Multi-GPU DCP inference was not exercised end-to-end, only cache-manager-level tests.

**Key insights**  
The change is well-scoped and backward compatible: the new field is optional everywhere, defaults to None, and legacy payloads decode cleanly. Test coverage spans Python config hashing, cache-manager DCP=1/4 values, startup synchronization, rank disagreement, MessagePack fixtures, and gRPC control behavior. The main review focus should be on the aggregation semantics (all-engines-must-agree) and whether the Python client's monotonic set accumulation is the intended long-term behavior.

## 70. [[Bugfix][Frontend] Lazy-import model_hosting_container_standards to prevent log suppression](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51366)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #51366
- **Author:** [wenjinhust](https://github.com/wenjinhust)
- **Merged By:** [ZJY0516](https://github.com/ZJY0516)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 01:25:49
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51366/files) (2):**
  - `vllm/entrypoints/serve/lora/api_router.py`
  - `vllm/entrypoints/serve/sagemaker/api_router.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes Bug #53655 where importing model_hosting_container_standards.sagemaker at module level triggered configure_root_logger(), setting shared root/vllm handler levels to ERROR and suppressing vLLM startup INFO/WARNING logs. The fix defers these imports into the functions that actually use them (attach_router, _attach_router, sagemaker_standards_bootstrap) and snapshots/restores handler levels around the import to prevent side effects from leaking.

**Technical impact**  
Changes import timing and adds handler-level snapshot/restore logic in two API router modules (lora and sagemaker). Behavior of the LoRA and SageMaker endpoints is preserved, but logging configuration is now protected from third-party reconfiguration during import. Adds helper functions _snapshot_handler_levels and _restore_handler_levels, and splits attach_router into a wrapper plus _attach_router.

**Potential risks**  
The snapshot/restore only covers handlers present on the root and 'vllm' loggers at snapshot time; handlers added or modified by the third-party import after the snapshot, or loggers other than root/vllm, are not protected. Deduplication by id(handler) is correct but relies on handler identity. If model_hosting_container_standards legitimately intends to configure logging, this fix may mask intended behavior. Lazy imports could surface import errors later at call time rather than at module load, changing failure timing.

**Key insights**  
The root cause is an import-time side effect in a third-party package that mutates shared logging handlers. The chosen mitigation (lazy import plus snapshot/restore) is a pragmatic workaround rather than a fix in the offending library. The PR includes a concrete reproduction test verifying handler levels remain INFO, which is good evidence, but no automated regression test appears to be added to the repo.

## 71. [[Parser] Fix: correct parser frontend handling of reasoning end and boundary tokens](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56635)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56635
- **Author:** [devtyagi3909](https://github.com/devtyagi3909)
- **Merged By:** [ywang96](https://github.com/ywang96)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 01:17:23
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56635/files) (3):**
  - `tests/parser/engine/test_glm47_moe.py`
  - `vllm/parser/engine/parser_engine.py`
  - `vllm/parser/glm47_moe.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes two parser frontend backward-scan bugs: extract_content_ids dropped content on mixed deltas containing both tool calls and think ends, and is_reasoning_end could falsely report True for a fresh turn lacking boundary guards. The fix centralizes logic in parser_engine.py by using reasoning_end_token_ids and turn_boundary_tokens, adds GLM_TURN_BOUNDARIES for glm47_moe, and removes now-redundant manual overrides in Glm47MoeParser and Qwen3Parser.

**Technical impact**  
extract_content_ids now respects wait_for_reasoning and turn boundaries, returning content from the correct turn start rather than the last matching end token across the whole sequence. is_reasoning_end gains boundary awareness so earlier-turn markers no longer leak into current-turn state. Behavior changes affect GLM-4.7 and Qwen3 parsers, with new tests covering reasoning-to-tool-call transitions and multi-turn prompts.

**Potential risks**  
Removing wait_for_reasoning=thinking from glm47_moe_config relies on initial_state to derive the flag; if any caller sets initial_state inconsistently, reasoning gating could regress. The new backward scan over turn boundaries adds O(n) work per call and assumes boundary tokens are correctly tokenized. Qwen3Parser override removal is not shown in the diff, so its behavior change is unverified here.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is that reasoning-end detection must be scoped to the current turn, not the entire token history, which the turn_boundary_tokens mechanism now enforces. Centralizing the scan in the base engine removes duplicated overrides and makes the tool-call-as-implicit-reasoning-terminator path explicit. The added tests are well-targeted at the exact regression scenarios described.

## 72. [[Bugfix] tolerate malformed EXIF metadata during hashing (#56527)](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56576)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56576
- **Author:** [amasen02](https://github.com/amasen02)
- **Merged By:** [DarkLight1337](https://github.com/DarkLight1337)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 00:56:51
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56576/files) (2):**
  - `tests/multimodal/test_hasher.py`
  - `vllm/multimodal/hasher.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes issue #56527 where images with malformed or corrupt EXIF segments caused unhandled exceptions (e.g. SyntaxError from invalid TIFF headers) during multimodal hashing, resulting in HTTP 500 errors. A new helper _get_image_id_bytes() wraps EXIF ImageID extraction in a try/except Exception block, falling back to standard pixel hashing for Image.Image or original_bytes for MediaWithBytes when EXIF parsing fails.

**Technical impact**  
The hasher now tolerates malformed EXIF metadata consistently with normalize_image's existing error handling, preventing API server request aborts. Valid EXIF ImageID UUID extraction behavior is preserved, and the refactor consolidates duplicated EXIF logic from two branches into a single helper. A new unit test covers both Image.Image and MediaWithBytes paths with a crafted malformed APP1 segment.

**Potential risks**  
The broad except Exception clause could silently mask unexpected errors beyond EXIF parsing issues, though this matches the intended tolerance pattern. The fallback changes the hash value for images with malformed EXIF compared to a hypothetical successful parse, but since parsing previously raised, no prior valid behavior is broken. Test relies on constructing a specific malformed JPEG structure that could be brittle across Pillow versions.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was that SyntaxError does not inherit from ValueError, so it escaped the existing error handling. The fix correctly centralizes EXIF ImageID extraction and applies defensive exception handling at the hashing layer. The test validates both the direct Image.Image path and the MediaWithBytes wrapper path, ensuring comprehensive coverage of the two affected code branches.

## 73. [[Bugfix][CPU] Add per-tensor FP8 W8A16 kernel to fix Ministral crash on CPU](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56985)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #56985
- **Author:** [bigPYJ1151](https://github.com/bigPYJ1151)
- **Merged By:** [bigPYJ1151](https://github.com/bigPYJ1151)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 00:49:17
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/56985/files) (6):**
  - `.buildkite/scripts/hardware_ci/run-cpu-test.sh`
  - `tests/kernels/quantization/test_cpu_fp8_scaled_mm.py`
  - `tests/v1/e2e/test_cpu_spec_decode.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/scaled_mm/__init__.py`
  - `vllm/model_executor/kernels/linear/scaled_mm/cpu.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a new CPUFp8PerTensorScaledMMLinearKernel that implements per-tensor FP8 W8A16 GEMM on CPU by reusing the existing AMX block-scaled kernel with a synthetic single-block scale, and wires it into the CPU backend kernel list so fp8-quantized models like Ministral-3-8B no longer crash on load. It also fixes two CPU CI issues: extending the offline-to-online retry pattern to match default_loader.py's 'Cannot find any model weights with' RuntimeError, and mounting ~/.cache/vllm so multimodal test assets persist across container runs. Tests were added for the new kernel and a spec-decode test model ID was corrected.

**Technical impact**  
Introduces a new kernel class that transposes the [K, N] weight back to [N, K], VNNI-packs it, and synthesizes a block-scale tensor so fp8_scaled_mm_cpu can be reused without FP8 activation quantization. It is registered ahead of PerTensorTorchFP8ScaledMMLinearKernel in the CPU backend resolution order, so on AMX-capable CPUs it will be selected for per-tensor FP8 configs, changing which kernel executes for those models. The CI changes alter offline/online fallback behavior and add a persistent cache mount, affecting test reliability and network usage.

**Potential risks**  
The synthetic block-scale trick assumes the AMX kernel's block semantics match a single per-tensor scale; any mismatch in block_size_n rounding or num_k_blocks could silently produce wrong results for shapes not covered by tests. The N-multiple-of-32 restriction is enforced in can_implement, but other unsupported shapes (e.g. K not aligned to 128) are not explicitly rejected and could fall through to the underlying kernel. Reordering the CPU backend list may change behavior for existing per-tensor FP8 workloads that previously used the torch kernel. CI pattern matching is string-based and could over-match unrelated errors, and the new cache mount may mask genuine cache-miss regressions.

**Key insights**  
The fix is pragmatic: rather than writing a new AMX kernel, it adapts the block-scaled path to per-tensor scaling, which keeps the change small but couples correctness to the underlying kernel's block assumptions. The added tests cover the weight-orientation fixup and the N%32 rejection path, which are the most likely failure modes. The CI changes are independent reliability fixes bundled in, and reviewers should verify the retry pattern does not swallow legitimate failures and that the kernel selection order change is intentional for all CPU FP8 per-tensor cases.

## 74. [[Bugfix][KV Offload] Track cache recency once per request](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51787)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #51787
- **Author:** [mindungil](https://github.com/mindungil)
- **Merged By:** [orozery](https://github.com/orozery)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 00:47:27
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51787/files) (10):**
  - `tests/v1/kv_connector/unit/offloading_connector/test_scheduler.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/cpu/test_manager.py`
  - `tests/v1/kv_offload/tiering/test_tiering_offloading.py`
  - `vllm/distributed/kv_transfer/kv_connector/v1/offloading/scheduler.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/base.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/cpu/manager.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/cpu/policies/arc.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/cpu/policies/base.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/cpu/policies/lru.py`
  - `vllm/v1/kv_offload/tiering/manager.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
This PR fixes a bug in the KV offload scheduler where the full cached prefix was re-touched on every decode step, causing frequency-based policies like ARC to count a single request as many accesses, and where transfer-pinned LRU blocks re-entered the evictable set in completion order, potentially evicting an early prefix block and leaving an unusable cached suffix. The fix removes scheduler-driven touch calls, records loaded/offered keys with their end-token positions in ReqContext, and applies one logical head-to-tail cache access in on_request_finished independent of transfer completion order. It also reworks ARC to keep new entries in T1 and count reuse once, gives LRU a logical recency rank separate from physical pinning with lazy-heap compaction, and merges token positions across hybrid KV groups so tails evict before heads globally.

**Technical impact**  
The change touches the offloading scheduler, CPU manager, ARC and LRU cache policies, the tiering manager, and the base ReqContext/CachePolicy interfaces. A new on_request_finished hook and on_store_miss hook are added to CachePolicy with default implementations for backward compatibility, and the existing touch API is retained. LRU now uses a lazy-invalidating min-heap with rank-based ordering and atomic batch eviction, while ARC adapts to B1/B2 ghost hits once per request before insertion. Tiering cascade reads are treated as internal pins rather than request accesses, and a cache generation counter invalidates stale request access state after reset_cache.

**Potential risks**  
The LRU heap compaction and lazy invalidation logic is subtle and could regress eviction correctness or performance under heavy pin/unpin churn if the compaction thresholds are mis-tuned. The new on_request_finished hook changes when recency is committed, so any out-of-tree policy relying on the old touch timing may behave differently despite the default implementation. The cache generation mechanism must correctly discard stale state across reset_cache, and the assertion that no new keys are observed after finalization could fire in unexpected scheduler paths. The PR notes the GPU E2E test used a nightly image base with an overlaid scheduler diff, so full integration on the exact target commit is not directly verified.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is that cache recency should be a logical, request-scoped property committed at request finalization rather than a physical, transfer-completion-ordered side effect, which decouples eviction correctness from asynchronous I/O ordering. Separating logical recency rank from physical pinning in LRU, and distinguishing insertion-only keys from reused keys in ARC, are the key mechanisms that make one-access-per-request accounting possible. The test suite is extensive (303 unit tests plus a GPU E2E) and specifically covers late completion ordering, atomic batch eviction, and ghost-hit adaptation, which gives reasonable confidence in the fix.

---

## 75. [[ROCm][AITER] Skip AITER norm kernels when flattening to 2D would copy](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55991)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #55991
- **Author:** [ZhengGong-amd](https://github.com/ZhengGong-amd)
- **Merged By:** [tjtanaa](https://github.com/tjtanaa)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 00:26:56
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55991/files) (2):**
  - `tests/kernels/ir/test_aiter_norm_dispatch.py`
  - `vllm/kernels/aiter_ops.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Adds a flatten_to_2d_is_free predicate to vllm/kernels/aiter_ops.py and requires it in the AITER rms_norm and fused_add_rms_norm support predicates, so AITER norm kernels are skipped when Tensor.reshape of a >2-D activation would silently materialize a contiguous copy. This targets per-head QK norm on fused-QKV slices, where the hidden copy costs more than the kernel saves, and falls through to the native Inductor provider instead. A new test file validates the predicate against reshape storage-pointer behavior.

**Technical impact**  
Changes dispatch selection for AITER norm ops on ROCm: rank>2 inputs whose leading dims cannot be merged without a copy now route to native/Inductor, which consumes arbitrary strides directly. Contiguous rank-3 inputs and 2-D inputs still dispatch to AITER, so the guard is layout-specific rather than rank-specific. The predicate runs once per graph node during VllmIRLoweringPass, not per forward, so steady-state overhead is negligible. Reported end-to-end gains are +2.3% to +6.9% on affected models, with kernel-level speedups of 1.97x (gfx950) and 3.9x (gfx942) on the strided slice case.

**Potential risks**  
The predicate's stride logic is hand-rolled and must exactly match reshape's view-vs-copy semantics; subtle cases (size-1 dims, non-unit last-dim stride, negative strides) could diverge from PyTorch behavior, though the new tests cover several such layouts. Dispatch changes could alter numerics or fusion behavior on models not covered by the benchmark matrix, and the reported wins depend on Inductor reliably avoiding materialization for strided inputs. The control model (Qwen3.5) shows zero predicate calls reaching the guard, so the change is a no-op there, but broader model coverage is not exhaustively verified.

**Key insights**  
The core insight is that reshape silently degrades to contiguous() and the existing predicates only checked dtype and variance_size, so AITER was selected even when the flattening copy dominated the kernel savings. The fix is a cheap, layout-aware guard that preserves AITER for genuinely flattenable inputs while deferring to native for strided slices. The PR is well-instrumented with dispatch probes, kernel-level timing, and a no-op control, which substantiates the load-bearing assumption that Inductor handles arbitrary strides without copying.

---

## 76. [[Bugfix] Fix incorrect Mamba block allocation estimate that prevents request admission](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57050)


### Base Information

- **PR Number:** #57050
- **Author:** [wzhao18](https://github.com/wzhao18)
- **Merged By:** [ywang96](https://github.com/ywang96)
- **Merged time:** 2026-09-16 00:23:21
- **Type:** `SUBSTANTIAL`
- **[Changed Files](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/57050/files) (2):**
  - `tests/v1/core/test_single_type_kv_cache_manager.py`
  - `vllm/v1/core/single_type_kv_cache_manager.py`

### Summary

**What changed and why**  
Fixes a bug in MambaManager.get_num_blocks_to_allocate where the estimated physical block count for requests loading prefix cache from external KV storage (e.g., Mooncake) was computed incorrectly, causing long requests (e.g., Kimi K3 1M tokens) to fail admission and stall the scheduler. The fix replaces the previous conditional overwrite of num_new_blocks with a min() against a physical_block_cap that correctly accounts for checkpoint blocks and speculative blocks. A regression test was added in tests/v1/core/test_single_type_kv_cache_manager.py verifying that the admission estimate equals the actual allocated block count.

**Technical impact**  
Changes the admission-time block estimate for Mamba cache groups so it matches the subsequent physical allocation performed by allocate_new_blocks, unblocking requests that previously failed the admission gate. The new logic caps num_new_blocks at 1 + has_partial_hit + checkpoint_block plus num_speculative_blocks when either no blocks are allocated yet or a checkpoint block is involved, which is more conservative and consistent with actual allocation. This affects scheduler admission decisions for Mamba-based models with prefix caching and external KV connectors.

**Potential risks**  
The min() cap could under-estimate in edge cases not covered by the new test, potentially allowing over-admission if the actual allocation path diverges from the cap formula. The change interacts with checkpoint and speculative block accounting, so regressions could appear for configurations with num_speculative_blocks > 0 combined with partial prefix hits. Test coverage is limited to two block sizes and a few prompt lengths, so other Mamba cache modes or alignment settings may not be exercised.

**Key insights**  
The root cause was that the previous code overwrote num_new_blocks with a fixed formula instead of bounding it, and the speculative-block adjustment was applied in the wrong branch, producing an estimate that could exceed or mismatch the real allocation. Using min() against a computed physical_block_cap aligns the estimate with the allocation path and preserves the original num_new_blocks when it is already smaller. The added test directly asserts admission_estimate == allocation_estimate == allocated, which is the right invariant to prevent this class of admission/allocation mismatch bugs.
